{\rm Proj}_2 (\hat I(\tilde P^{\mathbb{P}} ) ) (A)  = \int_{\mathbb{R}^m} \int_{\mathbb{R}^m}  \mathbf{1}_{A}(y)  \hat I(\tilde P^{\mathbb{P}}) ({\mathrm{d}} x, {\mathrm{d}} y) =   \int_{\mathbb{R}^m} \int_{\mathbb{R}^m}  \mathbf{1}_A(y)  \pi_{x}( {\mathrm{d}} y)\mathbb{P}\circ X_T^{-1}({\mathrm{d}} x), 
\]
                                                                                                                                                                                                                             
for any Borel set $A \subseteq \mathbb{R}^m$.
Moreover, $I({\rm Proj}_2\tilde P^{\mathbb{P}})\in{\cal P}(\mathbb{R}^m)$ is given, for any Borel set $A \subseteq \mathbb{R}^m$, by
\begin{align*}
I({\rm Proj}_2\tilde P^\mathbb{P})(A)=\int_{{\cal P}(\mathbb{R}^m)} p( A) {\rm Proj}_2\tilde P^\mathbb{P}({\mathrm{d}} p)&= \int_{\mathbb{R}^m\times {\cal P}(\mathbb{R}^m)}p( A) \tilde P^\mathbb{P}({\mathrm{d}} x, {\mathrm{d}} p)\\
& = \int_{\mathbb{R}^m\times {\cal P}(\mathbb{R}^m)}\mathbf{1}_A( x,y) \delta_{\pi_x}({\mathrm{d}} p ) \mathbb{P}\circ X^{-1}_T({\mathrm{d}} x)={\rm Proj}_2 (\hat I(\tilde P^\mathbb{P}) ) (A).
\end{align*}
Thus, letting $\hat\mu_2(\mathbb{P}):={\rm Proj}_2 (\hat I(\tilde P^\mathbb{P}) )\in{\cal P}_p(\mathbb{R}^m)$, we have
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:def.Lambda}
 \tilde P^\mathbb{P} \in \Lambda( \mathbb{P}\circ X_T^{-1} , \hat\mu_2(\mathbb{P}) ) \eqqcolon \Lambda_T.
\end{equation}
Back in \eqref{eq.ms}, recall $\mathbb{P}\in {\cal P}_c(\mu_0,\nu)$ and $\hat\mu_2(\mathbb{P})\in {\cal P}_p(\mathbb{R}^m)$, we find that
\begin{align*}
&  \mathbb{E}^\mathbb{P} \bigg[ \int_0^Tf(s,X,u_s,\mu_s)\,\mathrm{d} s\bigg]+\langle Q_c\varphi, \mathbb{P}\circ X_T^{-1} \rangle +\varepsilon\\  
& \geq \mathbb{E}^\mathbb{P} \bigg[ \int_0^Tf(s,X,u_s,\mu_s)\,\mathrm{d} s\bigg] + \int_{\mathbb{R}^m}\int_{\mathbb{R}^m} \varphi(y)\pi_x({\mathrm{d}} y)\mathbb{P}\circ X_T^{-1}({\mathrm{d}} x) + \int_{\mathbb{R}^m}\int_{{\cal P}(\mathbb{R}^m)} c(x,p)\tilde P^\mathbb{P}({\mathrm{d}} x,{\mathrm{d}} p)  \\
 &\ge \mathbb{E}^\mathbb{P} \bigg[  \int_0^T f(s,X,u_s,\mu_s)\,\mathrm{d} s\bigg]  + \langle \varphi,\hat \mu_2(\mathbb{P})\rangle + \langle c,\tilde P^\mathbb{P}\rangle\\ 
 &\ge \mathbb{E}^\mathbb{P} \bigg[  \int_0^T f(s,X,u_s,\mu_s)\,\mathrm{d} s\bigg] + \langle \varphi,\hat \mu_2(\mathbb{P})\rangle+ {\cal W}_c(\mathbb{P}\circ X_T^{-1}, \hat\mu_2(\mathbb{P})) ,
\end{align*}
where the latter inequality follows by \eqref{eq:def.Lambda} and \cite[Lemma 2.1]{backhoff2019existence} which asserts that ${\cal W}_c(\mathbb{P}_T\circ X_T^{-1} , \hat\mu_2(\mathbb{P} )) =\mathop{\mathrm{inf}\vphantom{\mathrm{sup}}}_{\tilde P\in \Lambda_T}\langle c, \tilde P\rangle$.\medskip


Thus, since $\nu$ and $\mathbb{P}\in {\cal P}_c(\nu_{0},\nu)$ were taken arbitrary, we find that
\begin{align*}
\Psi^\varphi(\nu_{0})+\varepsilon & = \mathop{\mathrm{inf}\vphantom{\mathrm{sup}}}_{\nu\in{\cal P}_p(\mathbb{R}^m)} \mathop{\mathrm{inf}\vphantom{\mathrm{sup}}}_{\mathbb{P} \in {\cal P}_c(\nu_{0}, \nu ) }    \bigg(\mathbb{E}^\mathbb{P} \bigg[ \int_0^T f(s,X,u_s,\mu_s)\,\mathrm{d}  s\bigg] + \langle Q_c\varphi,\mathbb{P}\circ X_T^{-1}  \rangle\bigg) + \varepsilon\\
& \geq   \mathop{\mathrm{inf}\vphantom{\mathrm{sup}}}_{\nu\in{\cal P}_p(\mathbb{R}^m)}  \mathop{\mathrm{inf}\vphantom{\mathrm{sup}}}_{\mathbb{P} \in {\cal P}_c(\nu_{0}, \nu ) }  \mathbb{E}^\mathbb{P} \bigg[  \int_0^T f(s,X,u_s,\mu_s)\,\mathrm{d}  s\bigg] + \langle \varphi,\hat\mu_2(\mathbb{P}) \rangle + {\cal W}_c\big( \mathbb{P}\circ X_T^{-1}  ,  \hat\mu_2(\mathbb{P}) \big)  \\
& \geq   \mathop{\mathrm{inf}\vphantom{\mathrm{sup}}}_{\nu\in{\cal P}_p(\mathbb{R}^m)}  \mathop{\mathrm{inf}\vphantom{\mathrm{sup}}}_{\mathbb{P} \in {\cal P}_c(\nu_{0}, \nu ) }  \mathbb{E}^\mathbb{P} \bigg[  \int_0^T f(s,X,u_s,\mu_s)\,\mathrm{d} s\bigg] + \langle \varphi,\hat\mu_2(\mathbb{P}) \rangle  ,
\end{align*}
where the last step follows since ${\cal W}_c\geq 0$.
	Finally, since $\hat\mu_2(\mathbb{P})\in {\cal P}_p(\mathbb{R}^m)$, the choice $\nu=\hat \mu_2(\mathbb{P})$ leads to ${\cal P}_c(\nu_0,\hat \mu_2(\mathbb{P}))$.
	Thus, we deduce that
\begin{equation*}
\Psi^\varphi(\nu_{0})+\varepsilon  \geq \mathop{\mathrm{inf}\vphantom{\mathrm{sup}}}_{\mu\in{\cal P}_p(\mathbb{R}^m)} \mathop{\mathrm{inf}\vphantom{\mathrm{sup}}}_{\mathbb{P} \in {\cal P}_c(\nu_{0}, \mu ) }  \bigg( \mathbb{E}^\mathbb{P} \bigg[  \int_0^T f(s,X,u_s,\mu_s)\,\mathrm{d} s\bigg] +  \langle \varphi, \mu\rangle\bigg) =-V^\ast_c(-\varphi).         
\end{equation*}	

	That is, $V^\ast_c(-\varphi)=-\Psi^{\varphi}(\nu_{0})$.\medskip

	This shows the dual formulation for $V_c(\nu_{0},\nu_{T})$ with $\Psi^\varphi(\nu)$ defined by \eqref{eq:def.Phi}. 
	That $\Psi^\varphi$ can be expressed as an infimum over ${\cal P}_{\rm det}(\nu)$ is given by \Cref{lem:disint}.
	To conclude, note that thanks to \Cref{rmk.equiv} and \Cref{lem:disint}, we can deduce from \eqref{eq.rules.det.random} that $V_c^\ast=(V_c^{\rm det})^\ast$.
	Taking conjugates again and using Fenchel-Moreau theorem we have that $V_c=(V_c^\ast)^\ast=(V_c^{\rm det})^{\ast\ast}$ as desired.
	\qedhere
<!-- end proof-6 -->
<!-- proposition-2: body_md -->
\label{prop:lower.bound.particle}
	Under the conditions of {\rm \Cref{thm:parti.conv.N}}, 
	it holds
	\begin{equation*}
		\liminf_{N\to \infty}N^{-1}V^N_e(\nu_{0},\nu_{T}) \ge                                                                                                                      
		V_e(\nu_{0},\nu_{T}).
	\end{equation*}
<!-- end proposition-2 -->
<!-- proof-7: body_md -->
The proof of the convergence result is based on our convex dual representation.
	The $N$-particle problem with value $V^N_e(\nu_{0},\nu_{T})$ does not directly fall into the setting of \eqref{eq:problem} because the random variables $(X^{1}_T, \dots, X^{N}_T)$ are not necessarily independent with respect to $\mathbb{P}^{N,\boldsymbol{\alpha}}$.
	However, applying the same strategy as in the proof of Theorem \ref{thm:duality.gen}, (but rather relying on Fenchel--Moreau theorem applied to the function ${\cal M}_p(\mathbb{R}^m)^N\ni (\nu_{T}^1,\dots,\nu_{T}^N)\longmapsto \tilde V^N_e(\nu_{0}, \nu_{T}^1,\dots,\nu_{T}^N)$) with
	\begin{equation*}
		\tilde V^N_e(\nu_{0}, \nu_{T}^1,\dots,\nu_{T}^N)
	\coloneqq 
	\mathop{\mathrm{inf}\vphantom{\mathrm{sup}}}\Big\{
	J^N_e\big(\mathbb{P}^{N,\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\big):\;  
	\mathbb{P}^{N,\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\in \Gamma^N(\nu_{0}^{\otimes N}) ,\;
	 \mathbb{P}^{N\!,\boldsymbol{\alpha}} \circ (X^{i,N}_T)^{-1} = \nu_{T}^i, \; i= 1,\dots, N 
	                                                                                 
	 \Big\},
	\end{equation*}
	it follows that $\tilde V^N_e(\nu_{0},\nu_{T}^1,\dots,\nu_{T}^N)$ is proper, convex and lower-semicontinuous on $\sigma({\cal M}(\mathbb{R}^m)^N, C_{p}(\mathbb{R}^m)^N)$ and thus admits the dual representation
	\begin{equation}
	\label{eq:raw.dual.N}
		\tilde V^N_e(\nu_{0},\nu_{T}^1,\dots,\nu_{T}^N) 
		= 
		\sup_{\boldsymbol{\varphi} \in C_{p}(\mathbb{R}^m)^N} 
		\bigg( \Psi^{N,\boldsymbol{\varphi}}(\nu_{0}^{\otimes N})  
		- 
		\sum_{i=1}^N\langle \varphi^i,\nu_{T}^i\rangle \bigg)
		= 
		\sup_{\boldsymbol{\varphi} \in C_{b,p}(\mathbb{R}^m)^N} 
		\bigg( \Psi^{N,\boldsymbol{\varphi}}(\nu_{0}^{\otimes N})  
		- 
		\sum_{i=1}^N\langle \varphi^i,\nu_{T}^i\rangle \bigg),
	\end{equation}
         
   
                                   
                                                                         
     
                                       
   
  
where $\boldsymbol{\varphi}\coloneqq (\varphi^1,\dots,\varphi^N)$ and 
\begin{align}\label{eq:psi-phi.N}
		\Psi^{N,\boldsymbol{\varphi}}(\nu_{0}^{\otimes N})
		\coloneqq 
		\mathop{\mathrm{inf}\vphantom{\mathrm{sup}}}_{\mathbb{P}^{N,\boldsymbol{\alpha}} \in \Gamma^N(\nu_{0}^{\otimes N})}
		\mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{P}^{N,\boldsymbol{\alpha}}}\bigg[ 
		\sum_{j=1}^N\int_0^T\frac12\| \sigma^\dagger \alpha^j_t\|^2\,\mathrm{d} t 
		+ 
		\sum_{j=1}^N      
		\varphi^j(X_T^{j,N}) \bigg] .
\end{align}

Using $V^N_e(\nu_{0}, \nu_{T}) = \tilde V^N_e(\nu_{0}, \nu_{T},\dots,\nu_{T})$ gives the dual representation for $V^N_e$.
	With the representation \eqref{eq:raw.dual.N} of $V^N_e$, we can now prove the lower bound as follows:
\medskip
		
	
	Let $\varphi \in C_{b,p}(\mathbb{R}^d)$ be arbitrary.
	By \eqref{eq:raw.dual.N}, letting $\boldsymbol{\varphi} = (\varphi,\dots,\varphi) \in C_{b,p}(\mathbb{R}^m)^N$ we have 
	\begin{align*}
		\frac1NV^N_e(\nu_{0},\nu_{T}) 
		&\ge 
		\frac1N\Psi^{N,\boldsymbol{\varphi}}(\nu_{0}^{\otimes N}) 
		- 
		\langle\varphi, \nu_{T}\rangle\\ 
		& = \mathop{\mathrm{inf}\vphantom{\mathrm{sup}}}_{\mathbb{P}^{N,\boldsymbol{\alpha}} \in \Gamma^N(\nu_{0}^{\otimes N})}
		\frac1N\sum_{j=1}^N\mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{P}^{N,\boldsymbol{\alpha}}}\bigg[
		\int_0^T\frac12\|\sigma^\dagger \alpha^j_t\|^2\,\mathrm{d} t 
		+     
		\varphi(X_T^{j,N}) \bigg] 
		- \langle \varphi, \nu_{T}\rangle.
	\end{align*}

Noticing that $\sigma$ is constant, by \cite[Theorems 1 and 2 and Proposition 1]{djete2020mckean2} the first term on the right-hand side above converges to the value $\Psi^\varphi(\nu_{0})$ given by 
                                                                                                                                                            
	\begin{equation*}
                                    
    
		\Psi^\varphi(\nu_{0}) 
		= 
		\mathop{\mathrm{inf}\vphantom{\mathrm{sup}}}_{\mathbb{P} \in {\cal P}(\nu_{0})}\mathbb{E}^{ \mathbb{P}}\bigg[
		\int_0^T\frac12\| \sigma^\dagger \alpha_t\|^2\,\mathrm{d} t
		+ 
		    
		\varphi(X_T) \bigg]  
		.
	\end{equation*}
	Since $\varphi\in C_b(\mathbb{R}^d)$ was taken arbitrary, recall \Cref{thm:duality.gen}, this shows that
	\begin{equation*}
		\liminf_{N\to \infty}\frac1NV^N_e(\nu_{0},\nu_{T}) \ge \sup_{\varphi\in C_{b,p}(\mathbb{R}^d)} ( \Psi^\varphi (\nu_{0}) - \langle \varphi, \nu_{T}\rangle) = V_e(\nu_{0},\nu_{T}),
	\end{equation*}
	which is the claimed bound.
<!-- end proof-7 -->
<!-- lemma-3: body_md -->
\label{lemma.approx.potential}
Let $N\in\mathbb{N}$ and $\varepsilon>0$ be fixed.
	There are functions $\varphi^{1,N},\dots, \varphi^{N,N}\in C_{b,p}(\mathbb{R}^m)$ and a probability measure $\mathbb{Q}^N\in\Gamma^N(\nu_{0}^{\otimes N})$ with $\mathbb{Q}^{N}\circ (X^{i,N}_T)^{-1} = \nu_{T}$ for all $i=1,\ldots,N$ such that, letting $\boldsymbol{\varphi}^N = (\varphi^{1,N},\ldots, \varphi^{N,N})$, we have that
\begin{equation}
	\label{eq:maximizing.seq.particle}
		\frac{1}{N}J^N_e (\mathbb{Q}^N) 
		-
		\frac{\varepsilon}{N}
		\le 
		\frac{1}{N}V^{N}_e(\nu_{0},\nu_{T})
		\le 
		\frac1N\Psi^{N,\boldsymbol{\varphi}^N}( \nu_{0}^{\otimes N}) 
		- 
		\frac1N\sum_{i=1}^N\langle \varphi^{i,N}, \nu_{T}\rangle 
		+ 
		\frac{\varepsilon}{N}.
	\end{equation}
Moreover, there exists a measurable function $\psi^N: \mathbb{R}^{mN}\longrightarrow\mathbb{R}$ such that
\begin{align}\label{eq.QN.charac}
		\dfrac{{\mathrm{d}}   \mathbb{Q}^N}{{\mathrm{d}}  \mathbb{P}^N} 
		=
		\dfrac{{\mathrm{d}}    \nu_{0}^{\otimes N}}{{\mathrm{d}}   \mu^N}( \mathbb{X}_0) 
		\exp\bigg(
		- \sum_{i=1}^N     
		\varphi^{i,N}(X_T^{i,N}) 
		- 
		{\color{black} \psi^N(\mathbb{X}_0)} + \varepsilon
		\bigg)
\end{align}
satisfying,
\begin{equation}
	\label{eq:conv.3}
		\mathbb{E}^{ \mathbb{P}^N}\bigg[  
		\dfrac{{\mathrm{d}}    \nu_{0}^{\otimes N}}{{\mathrm{d}}   \mu^N}( \mathbb{X}_0)  
		\exp\bigg(
		- 
		\sum_{i =1}^N     
		\varphi^{i,N}(X_T^{i,N}) 
		- 
		\psi^N( \mathbb{X}_0) 
		+ 
		\varepsilon \bigg)\bigg] = 1.
\end{equation}
<!-- end lemma-3 -->
<!-- proof-8: body_md -->
The existence of $\varphi^{1,N},\ldots, \varphi^{N,N}\in C_{b,p}(\mathbb{R}^m)$ and $\mathbb{Q}^N$ as in the statement such that \eqref{eq:maximizing.seq.particle} holds follows by definition of $V^{N}(\nu_{0},\nu_{T})$ and \eqref{eq:raw.dual.N}, recall \eqref{eq.rewardentropy} and \eqref{eq:psi-phi.N}.\medskip

Let us argue the second part of the statement.
	It follows from \eqref{eq:maximizing.seq.particle} that
                  
                                       
                                 
       
                                                                            
                                         
                                                  
     
                                               
                                             
     
                                                           
     
                         
                 
                                                                           
		\begin{align}
	\label{eq:conv.1}
		\frac1NJ^{N}_e (\mathbb{Q}^N) 
		- 
		\frac{\varepsilon}{N}&
		\le 
		\frac1N\Psi^{N,\boldsymbol{\varphi}^N}(\nu_{0}^{\otimes N}) 
		- 
		\frac1N\sum_{i=1}^N\langle \varphi^{i,N}, \nu_{T}\rangle
		+ 
		\frac{\varepsilon}{N}.
	\end{align}

As in \cite[Lemma 3.5]{tan2013optimal}, and using the fact that $X^1_0,\dots,X^N_0$ are i.i.d., we see that 
	\begin{equation*}
	\langle \Phi^{N,\boldsymbol{\varphi}}, \nu_{0}^{\otimes N}\rangle = \Psi^{N,\boldsymbol{\varphi}}(\nu_{0}^{\otimes N})
	\mbox{ where for }\setBold[0.2] x \unsetBold\coloneqq (x^1,\dots, x^N),\,
		\Phi^{N,\boldsymbol{\varphi}}(\setBold[0.2] x \unsetBold)
		\coloneqq 
		\mathop{\mathrm{inf}\vphantom{\mathrm{sup}}}_{\mathbb{P}^{N,\boldsymbol{\alpha}}  \in \Gamma^N(\delta_{\setBold[0.2] x \unsetBold})}
		\mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{P}^{N,\boldsymbol{\alpha}}}\bigg[ 
		\sum_{j=1}^N\int_0^T\frac12\| \sigma^\dagger  \alpha^j_t\|^2\,\mathrm{d} t 
		+ 
		\sum_{j=1}^N      
		\varphi^j(X_T^{j,N}) \bigg].
	\end{equation*}
	

Consequently,
\begin{align*}
		\Psi^{N,\boldsymbol{\varphi}^N}(\nu_{0}^{\otimes N}) 
		&= 
		\mathop{\mathrm{inf}\vphantom{\mathrm{sup}}}_{\mathbb{P}^{N,\boldsymbol{\alpha}}  \in \Gamma^N(\nu_{0}^{\otimes N})}
		\sum_{i=1}^N\mathbb{E}^{ \mathbb{P}^{N,\boldsymbol{\alpha}}}\bigg[ \int_0^T\frac12\|  \sigma^\dagger \alpha^i_t\|^2\,\mathrm{d} t 
		+     
		\varphi^{i,N}\big (X_T^{i,N}\big ) \bigg]\\
		&=
		-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{m N}}\log \bigg( \mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{P}^N_{\setBold[0.2] x \unsetBold}}\bigg[\exp\bigg(- \sum_{i=1}^N     
		\varphi^{i,N}\big(X_T^{i,N}\big) \bigg) \bigg]  \bigg)\nu_{0}^{\otimes N} ({\mathrm{d}} \setBold[0.2] x \unsetBold) 
	\end{align*}
	where the latter equation follows by Girsanov's theorem, see, e.g., \cite[Lemma 4.3]{hernandez2025marginal} for details.
\medskip	

                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 
Thus, coming back to \eqref{eq:conv.1}, since $\mathbb{Q}^{N}\circ (\mathbb{X}_0)^{-1} = \nu_{0}^{\otimes N}$ and $\mathbb{Q}^{N}\circ (X^{i,N}_T)^{-1}                 
=\nu_{T}$ for all $i=1,\ldots,N$, letting $\mathbb{Q}^N_{\setBold[0.2] x \unsetBold}$ denote the r.c.p.d.~of $\mathbb{Q}^N$ with respect to ${\cal F}_0^N$, and thanks to \cite[Corollary 4.4]{hernandez2025marginal}, we deduce from \eqref{eq:conv.1} that
\begin{align*}
	\int_{\mathbb{R}^{m N}}\log\bigg( \mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{P}^N_{\setBold[0.2] x \unsetBold}}\bigg[
		\exp\bigg(
		- \sum_{i=1}^N     
		\varphi^{i,N}(X_T^{i,N})
		+ \varepsilon
		\bigg)\bigg] \nu_{0}^{\otimes N} ({\mathrm{d}} \setBold[0.2] x \unsetBold)
         
                                          
                                                                       
                                                                                  
		& \le \int_{ \mathbb{R}^{m N} } \bigg( 
		\mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{Q}^N_{\setBold[0.2] x \unsetBold}}\bigg[- \sum_{i=1}^N    
		\varphi^{i,N}(X_T^{i,N}) + \varepsilon \bigg] 
		- {\cal H}\big(\mathbb{Q}^N_{\setBold[0.2] x \unsetBold}|\mathbb{P}^N_{\setBold[0.2] x \unsetBold}\big) \bigg)\nu_{0}^{\otimes N} ({\mathrm{d}} \setBold[0.2] x \unsetBold)  .
\end{align*}
This shows that, for $\nu_{0}^{\otimes N}\text{--a.e.}\, \setBold[0.2] x \unsetBold$, $\mathbb{Q}^N_{\setBold[0.2] x \unsetBold}$ is optimal for the variational problem
	\begin{equation*}
	                              
		\sup_{\mathbb{Q}\ll   \mathbb{P}^N_{\setBold[0.2] x \unsetBold}}  \bigg(
		\mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{Q}}\bigg[-  \sum_{i=1}^N     
		\varphi^{i,N}(X_T^{i,N}) 
		+ 
		\varepsilon \bigg] 
		- {\cal H}\big(\mathbb{Q}| \mathbb{P}^N_{\setBold[0.2] x \unsetBold} \big) \bigg)
		=\log\mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{P}^N_{\setBold[0.2] x \unsetBold}}\bigg[\exp\bigg(-  \sum_{i=1}^N     
		\varphi^{i,N}(X_T^{i,N})+ \varepsilon\bigg)\bigg].
	 \end{equation*} 
The existence of $\psi^N: \mathbb{R}^{mN}\longrightarrow\mathbb{R}$ as in the statement follows as in the proof of \cite[Theorem 3.8]{hernandez2025marginal}.
                                                                                                                                                                                            
In fact, it is given by
\begin{equation*}
	\psi^N(x) = \log\mathbb{E}_x^{\mathbb{P}^N}\Big[\exp\Big(-\sum_{i=1}^N \phi^{i,N}(X_T^{i,N})+\epsilon\Big)\Big];
\end{equation*} 
and its measurability follows from the Markov kernel.
<!-- end proof-8 -->
<!-- proposition-3: body_md -->
\label{prop:rQN=Palpha}
Let $N\geq 1$ be fixed and $\mathbb{P}^N,\mathbb{Q}^N\in{\rm Prob}(\Omega^N)$ as in {\rm \Cref{lemma.approx.potential}}.
	Then, there are square-integrable and $\mathbb{F}^N$-progressively measurable processes $\boldsymbol{\hat \alpha} = (\hat \alpha^1,\dots, \hat \alpha^N)$ such that $\mathbb{P}^{\hat \boldsymbol{\alpha},N} = \overleftarrow{\mathbb{Q}}^N$.
	 	The processes $\hat \alpha^i$ satisfy the identity
	 	\begin{equation}
        \label{eq:rep.boldalpha.N}
	 		\hat \alpha^i_t = \sigma \sigma^\top\nabla_{x_i}\log \overrightarrow u^N(t,\mathbb{X}_{t}),\; {\mathrm{d}} t\otimes {\mathrm{d}}  \overleftarrow{\mathbb{Q}}^N\text{--a.e.} \text{ on } [0,T]\times\Omega^N
	 	\end{equation}
	 	where the function $\overrightarrow{u}^N$, given by
	 	\begin{equation*}
	 		\overrightarrow u^N(t,\boldsymbol{x}) 
			\coloneqq 
			\mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{P}^N}\big[
			\exp\big( 
			-
			\psi^N(\mathbb{X}_T) 
			+ 
			\varepsilon 
			\big) 
			\big| \mathbb{X}_{t} = \setBold[0.2] x \unsetBold \big],
	 	\end{equation*}
satisfies the Hamilton-Jacobi-Bellman equation                         
\begin{align}\label{pde.forwardun}
\partial_t \overrightarrow u^N + L^N \overrightarrow u^N=0, \; (t,\setBold[0.2] x \unsetBold)\in [0,T)\times \mathbb{R}^{m N}, 
	\; \overrightarrow u^N(T,\setBold[0.2] x \unsetBold)=\exp(-\psi^N(\setBold[0.2] x \unsetBold)+\varepsilon).
\end{align}
<!-- end proposition-3 -->
<!-- proof-9: body_md -->
First, note that given \eqref{eq.QN.charac} as in the proof of \cite[Theorem 3.11]{hernandez2025marginal} we deduce that
\begin{align}\label{eq.rQN.charac}
\dfrac{{\mathrm{d}} \overleftarrow {  \mathbb{Q}}^N}{{\mathrm{d}}\overleftarrow{  \mathbb{P}}^N} = \dfrac{{\mathrm{d}}    \mathbb{Q}^{N}_T}{{\mathrm{d}}   \mu^N}(\overleftarrow \mathbb{X}_T) 
		\exp\Big(
		- \sum_{i=1}^N     
		\varphi^{i,N}(\overleftarrow X_T^{i,N}) 
		- 
		{\color{black} \psi^N(\overleftarrow \mathbb{X}_0)} + \varepsilon
		\Big)
\end{align}

which, since $\mathbb{P}^N$ is reversible implies, see \cite[Remark 3.12]{hernandez2025marginal}, that
\begin{align}\label{eq.FBpotentials}
    \sum_{i=1}^N     
    \varphi^{i,N}(x^{i})
	=
	\log\Big( 
	\mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{P}^N}\Big[ 
	\exp\big( 
	-
	\psi^N(\mathbb{X}_0) 
	+ 
	\varepsilon\big)
	\big| 
	 \mathbb{X}_T= \setBold[0.2] x \unsetBold
	 \Big]\Big), \;    \mathbb{Q}^{N}_T\text{--a.e.}\, \setBold[0.2] x \unsetBold.
\end{align}

From \eqref{eq.rQN.charac} we have that 
\begin{align*}
\dfrac{{\mathrm{d}} \overleftarrow {  \mathbb{Q}}^N}{{\mathrm{d}}{  \mathbb{P}}^N } = \dfrac{{\mathrm{d}}    \overleftarrow\mathbb{Q}^{N}_0}{{\mathrm{d}}   \mu^N}(  \mathbb{X}_0) 
		\exp\Big(
		- 
		 \psi^N( \mathbb{X}_T)
		- 
		\sum_{i=1}^N
		    
		\varphi^{i,N}(  X_0^{i,N}) 
		 + \varepsilon
		\Big).
\end{align*}

We now claim that $\overleftarrow{\mathbb{Q}}^N_{\setBold[0.2] x \unsetBold}$, the r.c.p.d. of $\overleftarrow{\mathbb{Q}}^N$, is optimal for the variational problem

\begin{equation}
	\label{eq:rvar.probl.particle}
		\sup_{\mathbb{Q}\ll   \mathbb{P}^N_{\setBold[0.2] x \unsetBold}}  \big(
		\mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{Q}}\big[
		- 
		\psi^N( \mathbb{X}_T) 
		+ 
		\varepsilon \big] 
		- {\cal H}\big(\mathbb{Q}| \mathbb{P}^N_{\setBold[0.2] x \unsetBold} \big) \big).
\end{equation} 

Indeed, it follows from classical results, for instance \cite[Section 4.9]{follmer2025stochastic}, that \eqref{eq:rvar.probl.particle} 
                                   
                
                                                                                                            
             
      
                  
      
                            
           
         
              
                                                 
    
                     
               
                  
      
                            
                
                                                
    
                                                 
                                                                                                      
                         
 has a unique solution $\mathds{Q}^N_{\setBold[0.2] x \unsetBold}$ given by
    \begin{align*}
\dfrac{{\mathrm{d}}  \mathds{Q}^N_{\setBold[0.2] x \unsetBold}}{{\mathrm{d}}{  \mathbb{P}}^N_{\setBold[0.2] x \unsetBold}} =                                                              
        \exp\Big(
        - 
         \psi^N( \mathbb{X}_T)
        - 
        \hat \varphi^{N}( \setBold[0.2] x \unsetBold) 
         + \varepsilon
        \Big)
\end{align*}
where $\hat \varphi^{N}$ is uniquely defined by
\[
\mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{P}^N_{\setBold[0.2] x \unsetBold}}\Big[ 
    \exp\big( - 
         \psi^N( \mathbb{X}_T)
        - 
        \hat \varphi^{N}( \setBold[0.2] x \unsetBold) 
         + \varepsilon\big)
         \Big]=1.                                  
\]
Putting $\mathds{Q}^N(\cdot) = \int_{\mathbb{R}^m }\mathds{Q}^N_{\setBold[0.2] x \unsetBold}(\cdot)\overleftarrow{\mathbb{Q}}^N_0({\mathrm{d}}\setBold[0.2] x \unsetBold)$, it follows by \eqref{eq.rQN.charac} and \eqref{eq.FBpotentials}, that $\mathds{Q}^N = \overleftarrow { \mathbb{Q}}^N$ and that $\hat \varphi^{N}(\setBold[0.2] x \unsetBold)=\sum_{i=1}^N    
\varphi^{i,N}(x^{i}), \mathbb{Q}^{N}_T\text{--a.e.}\, \setBold[0.2] x \unsetBold $ as desired.
This proves the claim.

	\medskip
	
It follows from \eqref{eq:rvar.probl.particle}, and Girsanov's theorem, that $\overleftarrow{\mathbb{Q}}^N_{\setBold[0.2] x \unsetBold}$ is optimal for 
\[
\sup_{\mathbb{P}^{N,\boldsymbol{\alpha}}  \in \Gamma^N(\delta_{\setBold[0.2] x \unsetBold})}
		\mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{P}^{N,\boldsymbol{\alpha}}}\bigg[
		            
		- 
		\psi^N( \mathbb{X}_T) 
		+ 
		\varepsilon
		        
		- 
		\frac12\int_0^T\sum_{i=1}^N\|\sigma^\dagger \alpha^i_t\|^2\,\mathrm{d} t
		\bigg] 
		=
		\log\Big( 
		\mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{P}_{\boldsymbol{x}}^N}\big[
		\exp\big( 
		-  
		\psi^N( \mathbb{X}_T)
		+ 
		\varepsilon
		\big)\big] \Big)
		=
		\log\big(
		\overrightarrow u (0,\setBold[0.2] x \unsetBold)\big)
\]
where the equality for $\overrightarrow u $ in the statement follows from \cite[Section 5]{leonard2011stochastic}.
	The assertion on $\hat\boldsymbol{\alpha}$ follows from classical verification arguments since $\overrightarrow u $ is a classical solution to \eqref{pde.forwardun}.
<!-- end proof-9 -->
<!-- proposition-4: body_md -->
\label{pro:H1bound.psiN}
		Let $N\geq 1$, $\varphi^{1,N},\dots, \varphi^{N,N}$ be as in {\rm \Cref{lemma.approx.potential}}.
	We have that
		\begin{equation*}
			\sup_{N\ge1}\frac1N\sum_{i=1}^N\|     
			\varphi^{i,N}\|^2_{H^1(\mathbb{R}^m,\nu_{T})}<+\infty.
		\end{equation*}
<!-- end proposition-4 -->
<!-- proof-10: body_md -->
Let us define
\(
\overrightarrow v^N(t,\setBold[0.2] x \unsetBold)\coloneqq\log (\overrightarrow u^N(t,\setBold[0.2] x \unsetBold)),
\)
and note that by \eqref{eq.FBpotentials}, \eqref{pde.forwardun} and the reversibility of $\mathbb{P}^N$, we have that
\begin{align}\label{eq.phi.equal.v}
    \sum_{i=1}^N     
    \varphi^{i,N}(x^{i}) =\overrightarrow v^N(0,\setBold[0.2] x \unsetBold), \;    \mathbb{Q}^{N}_T\text{--a.e.}\, \setBold[0.2] x \unsetBold.
\end{align}

Moreover, by \eqref{pde.forwardun} and the chain rule, the function $\overrightarrow v^N$ solves the equation
\begin{align}\label{pde.forwardvn}
	 \partial_t \overrightarrow v^N  +L^N \overrightarrow v^N + \Gamma(\overrightarrow v^N)=0,\;
	\overrightarrow v^N(T,\boldsymbol{x}) =-\psi^N(\setBold[0.2] x \unsetBold) +\varepsilon.  
\end{align}

Let us now consider the Fisher information
	\[
	I_t^N 
	\coloneqq 
	\mathbb{E}^{\overleftarrow \mathbb{Q}^N}\big[ \|\nabla \log \overrightarrow{u}^N(t, \mathbb{X}_t))\|^2 \big]
	= 
	\mathbb{E}^{\overleftarrow \mathbb{Q}^N}\big[ \|\nabla  \overrightarrow{v}^N(t, \mathbb{X}_t))\|^2 \big].
	\]
Now, we have that there is a constant $C>0$ such that
\begin{align}
	\notag
		C &\ge   \frac1N \big( J^N_e(\mathbb{Q}^N)+ {\cal H}(\nu_{0}^{\otimes N}|\mu^N)\big)= \frac1N {\cal H}( \mathbb{Q}^N|\mathbb{P}^N)  
		  = \frac1N  {\cal H}( \overleftarrow  \mathbb{Q}^N|\overleftarrow \mathbb{P}^N)  \notag \\
		 &= \frac1N \big( J^N_e(\overleftarrow \mathbb{Q}^N)+ {\cal H}(\mathbb{Q}^{N}_T|\mu^N)\big)\notag 
		  \geq  \frac1N \mathbb{E}^{\overleftarrow \mathbb{Q}^N}\bigg[\frac12\int_0^T\sum_{i=1}^N  { \| \sigma \sigma^\top \nabla_{x^i}\log \overrightarrow u^N(t,\mathbb{X}_t)\|^2} \,\mathrm{d} t \bigg] \notag \\
		& \geq   \frac1N  \frac{\Lambda}{2}\int_0^T I^N_t \,\mathrm{d} t  
		\label{eq:bound.I.vs.H}
\end{align}
where the first inequality follows from \eqref{eq:maximizing.seq.particle} and \eqref{assump.parti.conv.N}, the first equality follows, for instance, from \cite[Lemma 4.3]{hernandez2025marginal}, the second one holds since relative entropy remains constant under measurable maps, the third equality follows from \cite[Proposition 4.2]{cattiaux2023time}, the second inequality follows by the non-negativity of relative entropy and \Cref{prop:rQN=Palpha}, and the last one is due to \Cref{assump.Df}.\medskip

  
                                                                                                                                                                                                                       
                                                              
  
                                                                                                                                                                                                                            
               
                             
           
                                                                  
      
         
   
               
           
                                                                  
      
          
             
  

Let us set
\[
\overrightarrow{L}^{N,\overleftarrow \mathbb{Q}^N} f
	\coloneqq 
	L^Nf 
	+
	2 \Gamma(\overrightarrow v^N, f)
\] 
and define $\tilde I^N_t := \mathbb{E}^{\overleftarrow \mathbb{Q}^N}[\Gamma(\overrightarrow v^N(t, \mathbb{X}_t))] = \frac12\mathbb{E}^{\overleftarrow \mathbb{Q}^N}[\| \Sigma\Sigma^\top \nabla \overleftarrow v^N(t, \mathbb{X}_t)\|^2]$, which is the Fisher information up to the scaling $\Sigma\Sigma^\top$.
\medskip

By It\^o's formula applied to $F(t,\boldsymbol{x}):=\Gamma \overrightarrow v^N(t,\boldsymbol{x})$, we have
\begin{align}\label{eq.ito.scaledFisher}
		\tilde I^N_t- \tilde I^N_0 
		&= 
		\int_0^t 
		\mathbb{E}^{\overleftarrow \mathbb{Q}^N}\Big[
		\partial_t\Gamma(\overrightarrow v^N)(r,\mathbb{X}_r) 
		+ 
		\overrightarrow L^{N,\overleftarrow \mathbb{Q}^N}\Gamma(\overrightarrow v^N)(r,\mathbb{X}_r)
		 \Big]
		 \,\mathrm{d} r\notag \\ 
		& = 
		\int_0^t  
		\mathbb{E}^{\overleftarrow \mathbb{Q}^N}
		\Big[
		\partial_t\Gamma(\overrightarrow v^N)(r,\mathbb{X}_r) 
		+ 
		L^N\Gamma(\overrightarrow v^N)(r,\mathbb{X}_r) 
		+ 
		2 \Gamma(\overrightarrow v^N, \Gamma(\overrightarrow v^N))(r,\mathbb{X}_r) 
		\Big] 
		\,\mathrm{d} r.  
\end{align}

One can check that the carr\'e du champ satisfies $\partial_t\Gamma(f) = 2\Gamma(f, \partial_tf)$.
	Thus, substituting the PDE for $\overrightarrow v^N$ gives
\begin{align*}
\partial_t\Gamma(\overrightarrow v^N) 
 	=  
 	2\Gamma\big(\overrightarrow v^N, - L^N\overrightarrow v^N - \Gamma(\overrightarrow v^N) \big) 
	= -2\Gamma(\overrightarrow v^N, L^N\overrightarrow v^N) - 2\Gamma(\overrightarrow v^N, \Gamma(\overrightarrow v^N)).
\end{align*} 

Back in \eqref{eq.ito.scaledFisher}, it follows from the definition of the carr\'e du champ it\'er\'e that 
\begin{align*}
\tilde I^N_t- \tilde I^N_0  
	& =  
	\int_0^t  
	\mathbb{E}^{\overleftarrow \mathbb{Q}^N}\Big[
	L^N\Gamma(\overrightarrow v^N)(r,\mathbb{X}_r) 
	- 
	2\Gamma(\overrightarrow v^N, L^N\overrightarrow v^N)(r,\mathbb{X}_r) 
	- 2\Gamma(\overrightarrow v^N, \Gamma(\overrightarrow v^N))(r,\mathbb{X}_r) 
	+ 2 \Gamma(\overrightarrow v^N, \Gamma(\overrightarrow v^N))(r,\mathbb{X}_r)  
	\Big]
	\,\mathrm{d} r\\ 
	& = 
	\int_0^t 
	\mathbb{E}^{\overleftarrow \mathbb{Q}^N}\Big[ 
	2\Gamma_2(\overrightarrow v^N)(r,\mathbb{X}_r)
	\Big]
	\,\mathrm{d} r.
\end{align*}

Let us now recall that by \Cref{assump.Df}\ref{assump.Df.2}, we have                
\[
2 \Gamma_2(\overrightarrow v^N) 
	= 
	\frac12 \|\Sigma^\top    \nabla^2\overrightarrow v^N\Sigma \|^2 
	-
	\langle  \Sigma^\top \nabla \overrightarrow v^N ,\Sigma^\top  \nabla {\cal B}\,  \nabla \overrightarrow v^N\rangle 
	\ge 
	\frac12 \|\Sigma^\top  \nabla^2\overrightarrow v^N \Sigma \|^2  
	+ 
	\rho \|\Sigma^\top \nabla \overrightarrow v^N \|^2\geq 0.
\]
	
Hence, the Fisher information is increasing along the solution $\overrightarrow{v}^N$ of the Kolmogorov equation \eqref{pde.forwardvn}.
	In particular, we have $\tilde I^N_0 \le\tilde I^N_t $ for all $t\in [0,T]$, which, due to \eqref{eq:bound.I.vs.H} and \Cref{assump.Df}\ref{assump.Df.1} yields
	\begin{equation*}
	 \frac1N I^N_0
	 \le  
	 \frac1{N\Lambda} \tilde I^N_0
	 \le   
	 \frac{1}{N \Lambda T}
	 \int_0^T 
	 \tilde I^N_t 
	 \,\mathrm{d} t 
	 \le  
	 \frac{\|\Sigma\Sigma^\top\|_\infty^2}{N \Lambda T}
	 \int_0^T  
	 I^N_t 
	 \,\mathrm{d} t  
	 \le 
	 \frac{2C\|\Sigma\Sigma^\top\|_\infty^2}{\Lambda^2 T}.               
	\end{equation*}
Thus, $ \frac1N I^N_0$ is bounded. 
	Recalling {\color{black}$\mathbb{Q}^{N}\circ (X^{i,N}_T)^{-1} = \nu_{T}$ for all $i=1,\dots,N$} and \eqref{eq.phi.equal.v},
	it follows that
	                   
	                                                                                                                                                                                                         
	                 
    \begin{align*}
        \sup_{N\ge1}\frac1N\sum_{i=1}^N\int_{\mathbb{R}^m}\|\nabla     
        \varphi^{i,N}(x)\|^2\nu_{T}({\mathrm{d}} x) 
        &= 
        \sup_{N\ge 1}\frac1N\sum_{i=1}^N\mathbb{E}^{ \mathbb{Q}^N}[\|\nabla     
        \varphi^{i,N}(X^{i,N}_T)\|^2]\\
        &= \sup_{N\ge 1}\frac1N\mathbb{E}^{ \mathbb{Q}^N}[\|\nabla \overrightarrow v^N(0,\mathbb{X}_T)\|^2]\\
        &= \sup_{N\ge 1}\frac1N\mathbb{E}^{\overleftarrow \mathbb{Q}^N}[\|\nabla \overrightarrow v^N(0,\mathbb{X}_0)\|^2] \le \sup_{N\ge 1}  \frac{1}{N}I^N_0<+\infty.
    \end{align*}
                                                                                                                                                                    
	{Moreover, by Poincar\'e inequality and the fact that $\varphi^{i,N}$ has $\nu_{T}$-mean zero, there is a constant $C>0$, independent of $N$, such that}
	\begin{equation*}
		\|    
		 \varphi^{i,N} \|_{\mathbb{L}^2(\mathbb{R}^m,\nu_{T})} \le C \|\nabla     
		 \varphi^{i,N} \|_{\mathbb{L}^2(\mathbb{R}^m,\nu_{T})}.
	\end{equation*}
                   
         
                                                              
                                           
                 
      
                                                                                                                          
      
	This shows that $\sup_{N\ge 1}\frac1N\sum_{i=1}^N\|\varphi^{i,N}\|^2_{H^1(\mathbb{R}^m,\nu_{T})}<+\infty$ as desired.
<!-- end proof-10 -->
<!-- proposition-5: body_md -->
\label{prop.conv.phi^N}
                                          
Let $N\geq 1$, $\varphi^{1,N},\dots, \varphi^{N,N}$ be as in {\rm \Cref{lemma.approx.potential}}, $I_i\coloneqq [(i-1)/N, i/N),\, i=1,\dots N$, and let $\eta$ denote the Lebesgue probability measure on $[0,1]$.
		Let
	\begin{equation}\label{eq.phiN.lift}
		\bar\phi^N(x)\coloneqq\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^N \varphi^{i,N}(x), \; x\in \mathbb{R}^m,
		\mbox{ and, }
		\varphi^N:[0,1]\times \mathbb{R}^m\longrightarrow \mathbb{R}, \, \varphi^N(\theta, x) \coloneqq 
		\begin{cases} 
		\varphi^{i,N}(x) &  (\theta,x) \in I_i \times \mathbb{R}^m\\
		0 &\mbox{otherwise}.
		\end{cases}
	\end{equation}
                                                             
	Then we have:
	\begin{enumerate}[label=$(\roman*)$, ref=.$(\roman*)$,wide,  labelindent=0pt]
	\item The sequence $\varphi^N$ is bounded in $\mathbb{L}^2([0,1]; H^1(\mathbb{R}^m, \nu_{T}))$ and along a subsequence, $\varphi^N\longrightarrow\varphi$ weakly in $\mathbb{L}^2([0,1]; H^1(\mathbb{R}^m, \nu_{T}))$;
	\item $\bar\varphi^N\longrightarrow \bar\varphi$ with $\bar\varphi(x) \coloneqq \int_{0}^1\varphi(\theta,x)\eta({\mathrm{d}} \theta) $ weakly in $H^1(\mathbb{R}^m,\nu_{T})$ ;
	\item $\bar\varphi^N\longrightarrow \bar\varphi$ strongly in $\mathbb{L}^2_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathbb{R}^m, \lambda)$;
	\item $\bar \varphi^N \longrightarrow \bar\varphi$ strongly in $\mathbb{L}^p(\mathbb{R}^m, \nu_{T})$ for every $1\le p<2$;
	\item $\bar \varphi \in H^1(B_R,\lambda)$ for every $R>0$.
\end{enumerate}
<!-- end proposition-5 -->
<!-- proof-11: body_md -->
$(i)$: Let us first note that
	\[
		\sup_{N\ge1} \|  \bar\varphi^{N}\|_{H^1(\mathbb{R}^m,\nu_{T})}^2
        \leq 
       \sup_{N\ge1}\frac1N\sum_{i=1}^N\|     
			\varphi^{i,N}\|^2_{H^1(\mathbb{R}^m,\nu_{T})},
        \mbox{ and, }
       \sup_{ N\geq 1}\|\varphi^{N}\|_{\mathbb{L}^2([0,1]\times \mathbb{R}^m,\eta\otimes\nu_{T})}^2
       \le  
        \sup_{N\ge1}\frac1N\sum_{i=1}^N\|     
			\varphi^{i,N}\|^2_{\mathbb{L}^2(\mathbb{R}^m,\nu_{T})}.
	\]
	By \Cref{pro:H1bound.psiN}, 
                                                                                                                                                           
    it holds $\sup_{N\in \mathbb{N}}\|\bar \varphi^{N}\|^2_{H^1(\mathbb{R}^m,\nu_{T})}<\infty$.
    In particular, this shows that $\varphi^N$ is bounded in $\mathbb{L}^2([0,1]; H^1(\mathbb{R}^m, \nu_{T}))$ and thus that the sequence admits a converging subsequence in the weak topology.\medskip

    \item
	$(ii)$ and $(iii)$: We claim that $\bar\varphi^N, \nabla\bar\varphi^N  \in \mathbb{L}^2_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}^m,\lambda)$.
	In fact, under \Cref{assump.Df}\ref{assump.Df.5} there is a constant $C_R>0$ which does not depend on $N$ such that
	\[
	\int_{B_R}{\color{black}( \|\nabla\bar\varphi^N\|^2 +}  | \bar\varphi^N(x)|^2) \lambda({\mathrm{d}} x)=\int_{B_R} {\color{black}(\|\nabla\bar\varphi^N\|^2 +}| \bar\varphi^N(x)|^2) \frac{{\mathrm{d}}\lambda}{{\mathrm{d}} \nu_{T}}(x) \nu_{T}({\mathrm{d}} x)\leq C_{R} \int_{B_R} ( {\color{black}\|\nabla\bar\varphi^N\|^2 +} | \bar\varphi^N(x)|^2 )  \nu_{T}({\mathrm{d}} x)<+\infty.
	\]
	
	
	Now, recall that by Rellich-Kondrachov Theorem, see, e.g., \citet[Theorem 5.7.1]{evans1994partial} and the ensuing remark, the space $H^1(B_R, \lambda)$ is compactly embedded into $\mathbb{L}^2(B_R,\lambda)$.
	                                                                        
	Thus, there is a subsequence $N_k$, depending on $R$, such that the restriction to $B_R$ of $\bar\varphi^{N_k}$ converges in $\mathbb{L}^2(B_R,\lambda)$ to a function $\hat \varphi$.
	Using a standard diagonalization argument yields that we can find a subsequence again denoted $N_k$ such that $\bar\varphi^{N_k}$ converges to $\hat \varphi$ in $\mathbb{L}^2_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathbb{R}^m,\lambda)$.
	Moreover, since $H^1(B_R,\lambda)$ is a Hilbert space, it is reflexive, and thus boundedness in $H^1(B_R,\lambda)$ of $\bar\varphi^N$ implies that  it admits a subsequence converging in the sense of distributions.
	But since strong convergence implies convergence in the sense of distributions, it follows by uniqueness of the limit that $\hat \varphi \in H^1(B_R,\lambda)$ for all $R>0$.

	
	                                                                                                  
	                                                                                                                                                   
	                                                                                                                                                        
	                                                                                                                                                                 
	                                                
	
	\medskip

	On the other hand,
	again thanks to \Cref{pro:H1bound.psiN},
                                                                              
    we have that $\sup_{ N\geq 1}\|\varphi^{N}\|_{\mathbb{L}^2(\eta\otimes\nu_{T}, [0,1]\times \mathbb{R}^m)}<+\infty$.
	This implies that up to a subsequence, $(\varphi^N)_{N\geq 1}$ converges to a weak limit $                         
	\varphi \in \mathbb{L}^2([0,1]\times \mathbb{R}^m,\eta\otimes \nu_{T})$.
	Recall that the integral operator ${\cal I}:\mathbb{L}^2([0,1]\times \mathbb{R}^m)\longrightarrow \mathbb{L}^2(\mathbb{R}^m)$, $({\cal I} f)(x) \coloneqq \int_0^1f(\theta,x) \eta({\mathrm{d}}\theta)$ for $f\in \mathbb{L}^2([0,1]\times \mathbb{R}^m)$, is continuous and thus weakly continuous, and satisfies, ${\cal I} \varphi^N =\bar\varphi^N$.
	Hence, it follows that $\bar\varphi^N$ converges weakly to $\bar\varphi=\int_0^1                         
	\varphi(\theta,\cdot) \eta({\mathrm{d}}\theta)={\cal I} \varphi $.
	By uniqueness of the weak limit and Jensen's inequality, $\hat\varphi=\bar\varphi\in \mathbb{L}^2(\mathbb{R}^m,\nu_{T})$.
	                                                                                                                                                                                              
	                                                                       
\medskip



$(iv)$:    Let  $p\in [1,2)$.
    We now claim $\bar\varphi^{N_k}$ converges in $\mathbb{L}^p(\mathbb{R}^m,\nu_{T})$.
	Indeed, note that for every $R>0$, we have
	                                                  
	\begin{align}\label{eq.conv.phiN}
		\|(\bar\varphi^{N} - \bar\varphi)\1{B_R^c}\|^p_{\mathbb{L}^p(\mathbb{R}^m,\nu_{T})} 
		                                                                                         
		\le \sup_{N\geq1} \|(\bar\varphi^N - \bar\varphi)\|_{\mathbb{L}^2(\mathbb{R}^m,\nu_{T})} \nu_{T}(B_R^c)^{(p-1)/p}
		\le \frac{C+\|\bar\varphi\|_{\mathbb{L}^2(\mathbb{R}^m,\nu_{T})}}{R^{(p-1)/p}} \bigg(\int_{\mathbb{R}^m}  |x| \nu_{T}({\mathrm{d}} x)\bigg)^{(p-1)/p}                                                
	\end{align}
	where the first inequality holds by the definition of $\bar \varphi^N$ and Hölder's inequality, and the last inequality follows from Markov's inequality.
	Thus, using the triangle inequality, we have that
	\begin{align*}
		\|\bar\varphi^{N_k} - \bar\varphi\|_{\mathbb{L}^p(\mathbb{R}^m,\nu_{T})}^p &\le \|\bar\varphi^{N_k} - \bar\varphi\|^p_{\mathbb{L}^p(B_R,\nu_{T})} + \|(\bar\varphi^{N_k} -\bar \varphi)\1{B_R^c}\|^p_{\mathbb{L}^p(\mathbb{R}^m,\nu_{T})}.
		                                                                                                                                                              
	\end{align*}
	The claim follows from \eqref{eq.conv.phiN} and taking the limits subsequently in $N_k$ and $R$.\medskip

	$(v)$: This follows from $(ii)$ and (the proof of) $(iii)$.
<!-- end proof-11 -->
<!-- proposition-6: body_md -->
\label{prop:upper.bound.particle}
	Under the conditions of {\rm \Cref{thm:parti.conv.N}}, it holds
	\begin{equation*}
		\limsup_{N\longrightarrow  \infty}\frac1NV^N_e(\nu_{0},\nu_{T}) 
		\le 
		V_e(\nu_{0},\nu_{T}).
	\end{equation*}
<!-- end proposition-6 -->
<!-- proof-12: body_md -->
Starting from \eqref{eq:maximizing.seq.particle} we can write
	\begin{align}
	\notag
		\frac{1}{N}V^{N}_e(\nu_{0},\nu_{T}) 
		&\le  
		\frac1N\Psi^{N,\boldsymbol{\varphi}^N}( \nu_{0}^{\otimes N}) 
		- 
		\frac1N\sum_{i=1}^N\langle \varphi^{i,N}, \nu_{T}\rangle 
		+ 
		\frac{\varepsilon}{N}\\
		& \label{eq:maximizing.seq.particle.NEW} = 
		\mathop{\mathrm{inf}\vphantom{\mathrm{sup}}}_{\boldsymbol{\alpha} \in \Gamma^N(\nu_{0}^{\otimes N})}
		 \frac1N\sum_{i=1}^N\mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{P}^{N,\boldsymbol{\alpha}}}\bigg[ \int_0^T\frac12\|\sigma^\dagger \alpha^i_t\|^2\,\mathrm{d} t 
		 +
		     
		 \varphi^{i,N}(X_T^{i,N}) \bigg] 
		 - 
		 \frac1N\sum_{i=1}^N\langle \varphi^{i,N}, \nu_{T}\rangle 
		 + \frac{\varepsilon}{N}.
	\end{align}
	The goal of the next steps is to take the limit on the right hand side above.

	\medskip
	
	{\bf Step 1}. \emph{Reduction to bounded, Lipschitz controls}.
	Without loss of generality, we can assume that the infimum in the first term on the right-hand side above is taken over bounded processes $\alpha^i$.
	This can be achieved by approximating square-integrable controls with bounded ones.
	We can further reduce the infimum over Lipschitz, Markovian controls (i.e. $\alpha^{i}_t$ is a Lipschitz function of $\mathbb{X}_t$).
	In fact, let $\Gamma^N_{\mathrm{Lip}}(\nu_{0}^{\otimes N})$ be the set of elements of $\Gamma^N(\nu_{0}^{\otimes N})$ of the form $\boldsymbol{\alpha} = (\alpha^1,\dots,\alpha^N)$ with $\alpha^i_t = a^i_t(\mathbb{X}_t), t\in [0,T]$, for a Borel-measurable function $a^i:[0,T]\times \mathbb{R}^N\longrightarrow \mathbb{R}$ that is Lipschitz continuous in its second argument, uniformly in $t$.
	Let $\varepsilon>0$ and $\boldsymbol{\alpha} \in \Gamma^N(\nu_{0}^{\otimes N})$ bounded be such that
	\begin{align*}
		\frac1N\Psi^{N,\boldsymbol{\varphi}^N}( \nu_{0}^{\otimes N}) 
		\ge 
		\frac1N\sum_{i=1}^N\mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{P}^{N,\boldsymbol{\alpha}}}\bigg[ 
		\int_0^T\frac12\|\sigma^\dagger \alpha^i_t\|^2\,\mathrm{d} t 
		+ 
		    
		\varphi^{i,N}(X_T^{i,N}) 
		\bigg] 
		+ \varepsilon.
	\end{align*}
	By the mimicking theorem, \cite[Theorem 3.6]{brunick2013mimicking}, there is a filtered probability space $(\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\Omega}\overline{\phantom{\Omega}}$}\Omega, \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{{\cal F}}\overline{\phantom{{\cal F}}}$}{\cal F},\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\mathbb{F}}\overline{\phantom{\mathbb{F}}}$}\mathbb{F} ,\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\mathbb{P}}\overline{\phantom{\mathbb{P}}}$}\mathbb{P})$ that supports an $N$-dimensional Brownian motion $(\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{W}\overline{\phantom{W}}$}W^1,\dots, \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{W}\overline{\phantom{W}}$}W^N)$ and an adapted process $\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\mathbb{X}}\overline{\phantom{\mathbb{X}}}$}\mathbb{X} = (\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\overline{\phantom{X}}$}X^1,\dots, \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\overline{\phantom{X}}$}X^N)$ such that, with $a^i_t(\setBold[0.2] x \unsetBold) \coloneqq \mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{P}^{N,\boldsymbol{\alpha}}}[\alpha^i_t|\mathbb{X}_t = \setBold[0.2] x \unsetBold], t\in [0,T]$, $\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\mathbb{P}}\overline{\phantom{\mathbb{P}}}$}\mathbb{P}\circ \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\mathbb{X}}\overline{\phantom{\mathbb{X}}}$}\mathbb{X}^{-1}_t = \mathbb{P}^{N,\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\circ \mathbb{X}^{-1}_t$ for all $t \in [0,T]$, and
	\begin{equation}
		\label{eq:mimicking.sde}
		{\mathrm{d}}\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\overline{\phantom{X}}$}X_t^i 
		= 
		\big( a^i_t(\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\mathbb{X}}\overline{\phantom{\mathbb{X}}}$}\mathbb{X}_t)  + b(\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\overline{\phantom{X}}$}X_t^{i,N}, m^N_{\overline{\mathbb{X}}_t}) \big) \,\mathrm{d} t 
		+ 
		\sigma \,\mathrm{d}\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{W}\overline{\phantom{W}}$}W_t^i,\; \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\mathbb{P}}\overline{\phantom{\mathbb{P}}}$}\mathbb{P}\text{--a.s.} 
	\end{equation}
	Note that $a^i$ is a bounded measurable function.
	Let $\hat{\alpha}_t^i\coloneqq a^i_t(\mathbb{X}_t), t\in [0,T]$ and $\hat\boldsymbol{\alpha} := (\hat\alpha^1,\dots,\hat\alpha^N)$.
	Then, because under \Cref{assump.Df}\ref{assump.Df.2} the SDE \eqref{eq:mimicking.sde} has a unique weak solution, we have $\mathbb{P}^{N,\hat{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}}\circ \mathbb{X}^{-1}_t = \overline{\mathbb{P}}\circ \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\mathbb{X}}\overline{\phantom{\mathbb{X}}}$}\mathbb{X}^{-1}_t, t\in [0,T]$.
	Hence, by Jensen's inequality, we have
	\begin{align}\label{eq.mimick.estimate}
		 \frac1N\Psi^{N,\boldsymbol{\varphi}^N}( \nu_{0}^{\otimes N}) 
		&\ge 
		\frac1N\sum_{i=1}^N\mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{P}^{N,\boldsymbol{\alpha}}}\bigg[ 
		\int_0^T\frac12\|\sigma^\dagger a^i_t(\mathbb{X}_t)\|^2\,\mathrm{d} t 
		+ 
		    
		\varphi^{i,N}(X_T^{i,N}) 
		\bigg] 
		+ \varepsilon\notag \\
		& = 
		\frac1N\sum_{i=1}^N\mathbb{E}^{\bar{\mathbb{P}}}\bigg[
		\int_0^T\frac12\|\sigma^\dagger a^i_t(\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\mathbb{X}}\overline{\phantom{\mathbb{X}}}$}\mathbb{X}_t)\|^2\,\mathrm{d} t 
		+ 
		    
		\varphi^{i,N}(\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\overline{\phantom{X}}$}X^{i,N}_T) 
		 \bigg ]  
		 + \varepsilon\notag \\
		& =
		 \frac1N\sum_{i=1}^N\mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{P}^{N,\hat{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}}}\bigg[
		 \int_0^T\frac12\|\sigma^\dagger \hat{\alpha}^i_t\|^2 \,\mathrm{d} t 
		 + 
		     
		 \varphi^{i,N}(X^{i,N}_T) 
		  \bigg ]  
		  + \varepsilon.
	\end{align}
	Let us now approximate the function $a^i$ by standard mollifications $a^{i,\delta}$ as $\delta\downarrow 0$ and put
	\begin{equation*}
		\dfrac{{\mathrm{d}}\mathbb{P}^{N,\delta}}{{\mathrm{d}}\mathbb{P}^N} \coloneqq  {\cal E}\bigg(\sum_{i=1}^N\int_0^\cdot \hat \alpha^{i,\delta}_t\,\mathrm{d} W^i_t \bigg )_T\mbox{ with } \alpha^{i,\delta}_t \coloneqq a_t^{i,\delta}(\mathbb{X}_t).
	\end{equation*}
	
	Notice that, since $\hat\alpha, \hat\alpha^\delta$ are bounded, as $\delta\downarrow 0$ we have that $\sum_{i=1}^N\int_0^T\hat{\alpha}^{i,\delta}_t\,\mathrm{d} W_t^i$ converges to $\sum_{i=1}^N\int_0^T\hat{\alpha}^{i}_t\,\mathrm{d} W^i_t$ in any $\mathbb{L}^p(\Omega^N,\mathbb{P}^N)$, and then, $\frac{{\mathrm{d}}\mathbb{P}^{N,\delta}}{{\mathrm{d}}\mathbb{P}^N}$ converges to $\frac{{\mathrm{d}}\mathbb{P}^{N,\hat{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}}}{{\mathrm{d}}\mathbb{P}^N}$ in $\mathbb{L}^2(\Omega^N,\mathbb{P}^N)$.
	Thus, for any $i=1,\dots,N$, as $\delta \downarrow 0$ we have that $\mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{P}^{N,\delta}}[    
	\varphi^{i,N}(X^{i,N}_T)] \longrightarrow \mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{P}^{N,\hat{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}}}[    
	\varphi^{i,N}( X^{i,N}_T)]$, and, the estimate 
		\begin{align*}
		&\bigg|\mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{P}^{N, \delta}}\bigg[\int_0^T\|\sigma^\dagger \hat\alpha_t^{i,\delta}\|^2\,\mathrm{d} t \bigg] 
		- 
		\mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{P}^{N, \hat{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}}}\bigg[\int_0^T\|\sigma^\dagger \hat\alpha_t^{i,N}\|^2\,\mathrm{d} t \bigg]  \bigg| \\
		&
		 \le 
		\bigg| \Big(\mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{P}^{N, \delta}} - \mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{P}^{N, \hat{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}}}\Big)  
		\bigg[ \int_0^T\frac12\|\sigma^\dagger \hat{\alpha}^{i,N}_t\|^2\,\mathrm{d} t \bigg]  \bigg|
		+ 
		\frac12\bigg|\mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{P}^{N,\delta}}\bigg[\int_0^T\|\sigma^\dagger \hat{\alpha}_t^{i,\delta}\|^2 - \|\sigma^\dagger \alpha^{i,N}_t\|^2\,\mathrm{d} t \bigg ]\bigg|,
	\end{align*}
	together with the fact that $\frac{{\mathrm{d}} \mathbb{P}^{N,\delta}}{{\mathrm{d}}\mathbb{P}^{N}}$ is bounded in $\mathbb{L}^2(\Omega^N,\mathbb{P}^N)$, shows the convergence of costs.
	Consequently, back in \eqref{eq.mimick.estimate}, we have
	\begin{align*}
		\frac1N\Psi^{N,\boldsymbol{\varphi}^N}( \nu_{0}^{\otimes N}) 
		&\ge 
		\lim_{\delta\longrightarrow  0} \frac1N\sum_{i=1}^N
		\mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{P}^{N,\delta}}\bigg[ \int_0^T\frac12\|\sigma^\dagger \alpha^{i,\delta}_t\|^2\,\mathrm{d} t 
		+     
		\varphi^{i,N}(X_T^{i,N}) \bigg] + \varepsilon
	\end{align*}
	and therefore, since $\varepsilon>0$ was taken arbitrary,
	\begin{align*}
		\frac1N\Psi^{N,\boldsymbol{\varphi}^N}( \nu_{0}^{\otimes N}) 
		&\geq 
		\mathop{\mathrm{inf}\vphantom{\mathrm{sup}}}_{\boldsymbol{\alpha}\in \Gamma^N_{\mathrm{Lip}}(\nu_{0}^{\otimes N})} 
		\frac1N\sum_{i=1}^N
		\mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{P}^{N,\boldsymbol{\alpha}}}\bigg[ \int_0^T\frac12\|\sigma^\dagger \alpha^{i,N}_t\|^2\,\mathrm{d} t 
		+     
		\varphi^{i,N}(X_T^{i,N}) \bigg].
	\end{align*}
	And the above inequality is in fact an equality because the left hand side is clearly smaller than the right hand side.

	\medskip

	{\bf Step 2}. \emph{Terminal cost error.}
	                                                                                                   
	                                                                                                                                                                                              
	   
	               
	Consider the final dimensional dual functional
	\begin{equation*}
		D_N(\varphi^1,\dots, \varphi^N) := \frac1N\Psi^{N, \boldsymbol{\varphi}}(\nu^{\otimes N}_0) - \frac1N\sum_{i=1}^N\langle \varphi^i, \nu_{T}\rangle 
	\end{equation*}
	with $\boldsymbol{\varphi} = (\varphi^1,\dots, \varphi^N)$ and where $\Psi^{N, \boldsymbol{\varphi}}(\nu^{\otimes N}_0)$ defined in \eqref{eq:psi-phi.N} is the value of the unconstrained $N$-particle control problem with terminal cost $\sum_{i=1}^N\varphi^i(X^{i,N}_T)$.
	Thus \eqref{eq:maximizing.seq.particle} can be re-written as
	\begin{equation*}
		\frac1NV^N_e(\nu_{0},\nu_{T}) \le D_N(\varphi^{1,N}, \dots, \varphi^{N,N}) + \frac{\varepsilon}{N}.
	\end{equation*}
	Recall the averaged function 
	$$
		\bar\varphi^N(x) := \frac1N\sum_{i=1}^N\varphi^{i,N}(x).
	$$
	We claim that
	\begin{equation*}
		D_N(\varphi^{1,N}, \dots, \varphi^{N,N}) \le D_N(\bar\varphi^N,\dots, \bar\varphi^N).
	\end{equation*}
	In fact, for a permutation $\pi \in S_N$, the set of permutations of $\{1,\dots,N\}$, define $\varphi^N_\pi := (\varphi^{\pi(1), N},\dots, \varphi^{\pi(N),N})$.
	Because the initial law is $\nu_{0}^{\otimes N}$ and interaction is through the empirical measures, the control problem \eqref{eq:psi-phi.N} is invariant under relabeling of particles.
	Thus, $D_N(\varphi^N_\pi) = D_N(\varphi^{1,N}, \dots, \varphi^{N,N})$ for every permutation $\pi$.
	In addition, $D_N$ is concave.
	Indeed, because $\Psi^{N, \boldsymbol{\varphi}}(\nu_{0}^{\otimes N})$ is an infimum, it is easily seen to be concave (as a function) of $\boldsymbol{\varphi} = (\varphi^1,\dots, \varphi^N)$ and the term $-\frac1N\sum_{i=1}^N\langle \varphi^i,\nu_{T}\rangle$ is linear.
	Thus, by Jensen's inequality, 
	\begin{equation*}
		D_N\Big(\frac{1}{N!}\sum_{\pi \in S_N}\varphi^N_\pi \Big) \ge \frac{1}{N!}\sum_{\pi \in S_N}D_N(\varphi^N_\pi) = D_N(\varphi^{1,N}, \dots, \varphi^{N,N}).
	\end{equation*}
	But 
	\begin{equation*}
		\frac{1}{N!}\sum_{\pi \in S_N}\varphi^N_\pi = (\bar\varphi^N, \dots, \bar\varphi^N).
	\end{equation*}
	Thus, $D_N(\bar\varphi^N,\dots, \bar\varphi^N) \ge D_N(\varphi^{1,N},\dots, \varphi^{N,N})$ as announced.
	Therefore,                                                                              
	\begin{align*}
		\frac1NV^N_e(\nu_{0},\nu_{T}) &\le D_N(\bar\varphi^N, \dots, \bar\varphi^N) + \frac{\varepsilon}{N}\\
				                                                                                                                                     
				&\le \mathop{\mathrm{inf}\vphantom{\mathrm{sup}}}_{\boldsymbol{\alpha} \in \Gamma^N_{\mathrm{Lip}}(\nu_{0}^{\otimes N})}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^N\mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{P}^{N,\boldsymbol{\alpha}}}\bigg[\int_0^T\frac12\|\sigma^\dagger \alpha^i_t\|^2dt + \bar\varphi^N(X^{i,N}_T) \bigg ]	- \langle \bar\varphi^N, \nu_{T}\rangle + \frac{\varepsilon}{N}\\
				&\le \mathop{\mathrm{inf}\vphantom{\mathrm{sup}}}_{\boldsymbol{\alpha}\in \Gamma^N_{\mathrm{Lip}}(\nu_{0}^{\otimes N})} \frac1N\sum_{i=1}^N\mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{P}^{N,\boldsymbol{\alpha}}}\bigg[ \int_0^T\frac12\|\sigma^\dagger \alpha^i_t\|^2\,\mathrm{d} t + \bar\varphi(X^{i,N}_T)\bigg] - \langle \bar\varphi^N, \nu_{T}\rangle + \frac{\varepsilon}{N} + e^N.
	\end{align*}
	   
	                                                                                                                                                                                                                      
	                                                                
	                                                                                                             
	                                                      
	                                                                           
	                
	        
	                                                              
	      
	                                                                 
	                                                             
	                                           
	          
	                                         
	          
	                                                                  
	                                                            
	                                          
	         
	                                          
	              
	Where, for some $\boldsymbol{\alpha}_o\in \Gamma^N_{\mathrm{Lip}}(\nu_{0}^{\otimes N})$ we fixed, 
	\[
		e^N \coloneqq  \frac1N\sum_{i=1}^N\mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{P}^{N,\boldsymbol{\alpha}_o}}\big[    
		\bar h^N(X^{i,N}_T)\big],\;                                                                                                                                        
 \bar h^{N}(x)\coloneqq  \bar\varphi^{N}(x) - \bar\varphi(x).
\]

Now, since $\boldsymbol{\alpha}_o\in \Gamma^N_{\mathrm{Lip}}(\nu_{0}^{\otimes N})$, on                               
	the probability space $(\Omega, {\cal F},\mathbb{F}, \mathbb{P})$ supporting Brownian motions $(B^i)_{i\geq 1}$,\footnote{We may take $(\Omega^\infty, {\cal F}^\infty,\mathbb{F}^\infty, \mathbb{P}^\infty)$ the infinite product space induced by $(\Omega^N, {\cal F}^N,\mathbb{F}^N, \mathbb{P}^N)$.} the SDE
\begin{equation*}
	{\mathrm{d}}{\cal X}^{i,N}_t 
	= 
	\big( b({\cal X}^{i,N}_t, m^N_{{\cal X}_t}) + \alpha^{i,N}_o(t,{\cal X}^{1,N}_t,\dots,{\cal X}^{N,N}_t)\big) \,\mathrm{d} t 
	+ 
	\sigma  \,\mathrm{d} B^i_t, 
	\mbox{ with, } 
	m^N_{{\cal X}_s}\coloneqq \frac1N\sum_{i=1}^N\delta_{{\cal X}^{i,N}_s},
\end{equation*}
admits a unique solution.
	Thus, $\mathbb{P}^{N,\boldsymbol{\alpha}_o}\circ(X^{1,N},\dots, X^{N,N})^{-1}=\mathbb{P}\circ({\cal X}^{1,N}, \dots, {\cal X}^{N,N})^{-1}$. \medskip
   
                                                              
   
                                                                        
                   
                                                                                
                 
                                                                                                                                                                                                                  
                                                    
                 
        
        
                                                                                                               
      
                                                                                    
                            
               
                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               
                                            
            
                                               
                                                                                                                                  
                                                                                    
                                                                                                              
                         
            
          
                         
                                                                                                                              
         
       
                        
                                                                                                                               
                                                                       
         
                                                                                                                                                       
                
                                                                                                     
                                                                                 

Let $k>0$. We may then estimate the error term as
\begin{align*}\label{eq.errors}
		e^N 
		=  
		\frac1N\sum_{i=1}^N\mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{P}}\Big[\bar h^N({\cal X}^{i,N}_T)\Big]
		= 
		\frac1N\sum_{i=1}^N\Big(
		\mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{P}}\Big[ \bar h^N({\cal X}^{i,N}_T)\1{|{\cal X}^{i,N}_T|> k}
		\Big] 
		+ 
		\mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{P}}\Big[
		\bar h^N({\cal X}^{i,N}_T)\1{|{\cal X}^{i,N}_T|\le k}
		\Big] \Big)
		\eqqcolon 
		e^N_1 + e^N_2.
	\end{align*}
                                                                                                          
                                       

{\bf Step 3}. \emph{Estimating $e_1^N$.}
	By Cauchy--Schwarz and Markov's inequalities, we have 
	\begin{equation}\label{eq.e1}
		| e_1^N| 
		\leq 
		\frac{1}{Nk }
		\sum_{i=1}^N\mathbb{E}\big[
		|\bar h^{N}( {\cal X}^{i,N}_T)|^2
		\big]^{1/2}
		\mathbb{E}\big[\|{\cal X}^{i,N}_T\|^2\big]^{1/2}.
	\end{equation}

We claim that there is a constant $C>0$, independent of $N$ and $k$, such that
	\begin{equation}\label{eq.growth.hN}
   		\|\bar h^N\|_{\mathbb{L}^\infty( B_x, \lambda)}  
		\leq 
		C {\mathrm{e}}^{\kappa/2 \|x\|^2} 
		,\;  
		x\in  \mathbb{R}^m,
	\end{equation}
	where $\kappa$ is the constant in \Cref{assump.Df}\ref{assump.Df.5}.    
	Indeed, let $x\in\mathbb{R}^m$.
	It follows from \Cref{assump.Df}\ref{assump.Df.5} that
	\begin{align*}
    		\int_{ B_x }\|\nabla  \bar h^N(y)\|^2  \lambda ({\mathrm{d}} y) 
    		&\le 
    		C_\nu^{-1}{\mathrm{e}}^{\kappa(1 +\|x\|^2)}
    		\int_{ B_x }\|\nabla  \bar h^N( y)\|^2 \nu_{T} ({\mathrm{d}} y)\\
    		&\le
    		C_\nu^{-1}{\mathrm{e}}^{\kappa(1 +\|x\|^2)}\bigg(
    		\frac1N \sum_{i=1}^N
    		\int_{B_x} \|\nabla \varphi^{i,N}(y)\|^2 \, \nu_{T} ({\mathrm{d}} y) 
		+ 
		\int_{B_x} \int_0^1\|\nabla_{\! x} \varphi(\theta,y)\|^2\, \eta({\mathrm{d}}\theta)\nu_{T}({\mathrm{d}} y)
		\bigg).
	\end{align*}
	Thus, thanks to Propositions \ref{pro:H1bound.psiN} and \ref{prop.conv.phi^N}, there is $C>0$ independent of $N$ such that $ \|\nabla  \bar h^N \|_{\mathbb{L}^2(B_x, \lambda) } \le C {\mathrm{e}}^{\kappa(1 +\|x\|^2)/2}$.
    	Arguing similarly for $\bar h^N$ we obtain the bound
	\begin{equation*}
		\| \bar h^N\|_{H^{1}( B_x, \lambda)} \le C {\mathrm{e}}^{\kappa(1 +\|x\|^2)/2}.
	\end{equation*}
	Consequently, by the Sobolev embedding theorem \cite[Theorem 5.6.3.6]{evans1994partial}, there is $C>0$ independent of $x$ and $N$ such that
	\begin{align}\label{eq.growth.sumhN}
		\|\bar h^N\|_{\mathbb{L}^\infty( B_x, \lambda)} \le C\| \bar h^N\|_{W^{1,2}( B_x,\lambda )} \leq   C{\mathrm{e}}^{\kappa/2\|x\|^2},
	\end{align}
	where $C$ may have changed from one inequality to another. 
	This proves the claim \eqref{eq.growth.hN}.\medskip


                                                                                                          
                                     
                     
                                                                      
            

                                                                           
                                                                                                                                                                           
                                                                                                                                                
                                                                                                        
                                                                                
                                                                    
                                                                                                
                
                                                                                                                
                                                                                                                                                  
              
    
          
                                                                       
                                                                                         
                                                                                        
                                      
                                                                                   
               
                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             
                                                                      
                                                                                                                   
                                                        
               
                                                                                               
       
                                 
                                                                                                 
      
                                 
                      
                                                          
             
   
                                                                                                 
   
                                                                                                                                                                                                                           
                                                 
                                  
                                                                                              
               
                                                                                                                                             
               
                                                                                                                                                            
             
                                                           
                                              
 
      
                 
                                                                                   
                                      
                                                                                                            
               
 
                                                                         
                                                       
                                                           
               
                                                                                                  
          
                                    
                                                                                                    
         
                                          
                         
                                                                                                                                                 
             
                                                                                                                                                                                                                              
                                                    
                                  
                                                                                                             
               
                                                                                                                                             
               
                                                                                                                                                                                         
             
                                                           
                                            
                                                                                                                                                                                           
                                                                                                                                                                                                                
                                                                                               
                                                                                                                                                                               
       
               
                                                                                                                 
                                                                                                                                                    
             
  
                      
                                                                
                                                                                                                                                                                                               
                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     
               
                                                                                       
                                                                                                                                                                                                  
                                  
              


                                                                                                              
                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           
                                                                                                          
                                                                                                                                                           
                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     
                
                                                                                                                                                   
                                                                                                                 
                                                                                                
                                      
              
                                                                                                                                                                            


We now show that $({\cal X}^{i,N})_{i\leq N, N\geq 1}$ has uniform square-exponential moments of order $\kappa>0$ as in \Cref{assump.Df}\ref{assump.Df.5}. 
	To this end, observe that because the control $\boldsymbol{\alpha}_o$ is bounded, say by $C_A$, it follows from \Cref{assump.Df}\ref{assump.Df.2} that
                   
                                                                                                                                                                                
                 
                                            
	\begin{equation*}
		\|{\cal X}_t^{i,N}\|^2 
		\le 
		4\|{\cal X}^{i,N}_0\|^2 
		+ 4(\ell_b+C_A)^2T^2 
		+  8T\int_0^t \ell_b\Big( \|{\cal X}_{s}^{i,N}\|^2 + \frac{1}{N}\sum_{j=1}^N\|{\cal X}^{j,N}_s\|^2\Big) {\mathrm{d}} s 
		+ 4\|\sigma B^i\|_T^2, \; t\in [0,T],
	\end{equation*}
	where $\|B^i\|_T\coloneqq \sup_{t\in [0,T]} \|B_t^i\|$.
	Next, taking the average and using Gronwall's inequality, we find that
	\begin{align}\label{eq.estimate.average}
		\frac1N\sum_{i=1}^N\|{\cal X}_t^{i,N}\|^2 
		\le 
		4{\mathrm{e}}^{16\ell_b  T^2}
		\Big( \frac1N\sum_{i=1}^N \|{\cal X}^{i,N}_0\|^2 
		+ 
		(\ell_b+C_A)^2T 
		+ 
		 \frac1N\sum_{i=1}^N \|\sigma B^i\|_T^2 
		 \Big).
	\end{align}
                              
                                                                                                                  
                
                                                                                                                                                                                             
                                                                                                                               
               
	
Replacing this in the previous estimate and applying Gronwall's inequality again, letting $c\coloneqq 4(\ell_b+C_A)^2T^2  {\mathrm{e}}^{8\ell_b T^2}(1 + 8 \ell_b T {\mathrm{e}}^{16\ell_b T^2})$, we find that
                
                                                                                                                                                                                            
                                            
              
         
	\begin{align*}
		\|{\cal X}_t^{i,N}\|^2 
		\leq 
		c+  
		4{\mathrm{e}}^{8\ell_b T^2}
		\Big(\|{\cal X}^{i,N}_0\|^2  + 8\ell_b T^2 {\mathrm{e}}^{16\ell_b T^2} \frac1N\sum_{j=1}^N \|{\cal X}^{j,N}_0\|^2  \Big) 
		+ 
		4{\mathrm{e}}^{8\ell_b T^2}  
		\Big(\|\sigma B^i\|_T^2  + 8\ell_b  T^2{\mathrm{e}}^{16\ell_b T^2}\frac1N\sum_{j=1}^N \|\sigma B^j\|_T^2 \Big).
	\end{align*}
 
Since $\mathbb{X}^{i,N}_0$ and $B^i$ are independent, we deduce that for $c_1$ as in \Cref{assump.Df}\ref{assump.Df.5}                                                 
	\begin{align}\label{eq.e1.exp}
		\sup_{N\geq 1, i\leq N}\mathbb{E}\big[{\mathrm{e}}^{\kappa/2 \|{\cal X}^{i,N}_T\|^2}\big] 
		\le 
		{\mathrm{e}}^{\kappa c/2}
		\mathbb{E}\big[{\mathrm{e}}^{\kappa c_1\|{\cal X}_0\|^2}\big]
		\mathbb{E}\big[{\mathrm{e}}^{\kappa  c_1 \|\sigma B^1\|_T^2}\big]
		<+\infty,
	\end{align}	
	where ${\cal X}_0$ has law $\nu_{0}$. 
	Indeed, under \Cref{assump.Df}\ref{assump.Df.4}--\ref{assump.Df}\ref{assump.Df.5} both terms are finite since $\kappa c_1<c_0$ and $ \kappa \lambda_{\rm max}(\sigma\sigma^\top) c_1<1/2T$, see \cite[Corollary 3.2]{ledoux1991probability}.
	                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       
	\medskip
	
	From \eqref{eq.e1}, \eqref{eq.growth.sumhN} and \eqref{eq.e1.exp} we deduce that for any $k>0$ there is $C>0$, independent of $N$ and $k$, such that
	\begin{align}\label{eq.e1N.final}
	| e_1^N| \leq \frac{C}k.
	\end{align}
                          
                 
                                                                                
                                                                   
                   
                                                                                                                                   
                 
                                                                                                                                  
               

                                                                                           
                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            
                                                                                                                             
                
                                                                                                                                                               
                                                                                                               
                                                                                                               
              
                                                                                                                   
                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   

                                         
 
 
                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    

{\bf Step 4}. \emph{Estimating $e_2^N$.} 
	Let $R>k$ be fixed and 
	\begin{align*}
	\tau^{i,N}_R 
	\coloneqq 
	\mathop{\mathrm{inf}\vphantom{\mathrm{sup}}}\big\{t>0: 
	\|{\cal X}^{i,N}_t\| \vee M^N_t \ge R
	\big \}
	\mbox{ with } 
	M^N_t 
	\coloneqq 
	\Big(\frac1N\sum_{j=1}^N\|{\cal X}^{j,N}_t\|^2\Big)^{1/2}.
	\end{align*}
	Moreover, to alleviate the notation, let us write the dynamics of ${\cal X}^{i,N}$ as
	\begin{equation*}
		\,\mathrm{d} {\cal X}^{i,N}_t = \beta^{i,N}_t\,\mathrm{d}  t + \sigma \,\mathrm{d}  B^i_t, \mbox{ where, } \beta^{i,N}_t \coloneqq b({\cal X}^{i,N}_t, m^N_{{\cal X}_t}) + \alpha^{i,N}_o(t,{\cal X}^{1,N}_t,\dots,{\cal X}^{N,N}_t),
	\end{equation*}
	and note that                                                 
	$\beta^{i,N}_t$ is bounded uniformly in $i,N$ on $\{t\le \tau^{i,N}_R\}$. 
	Indeed, since $\| \alpha^{i,N}_0\|\leq C_A$, thanks to \Cref{assump.Df}\ref{assump.Df.2} we have 
	\[
	\|\beta^{i,N}_t\| \le \|b(0,\delta_0)\| + 2\ell_bR +A \eqqcolon  K_R,\; \mathbb{P}\text{--a.s.} ,\mbox{ on }\{t\le \tau^{i,N}_R\}.
	\] 

Let us now further decompose the error term $e^N_2$ as
	\begin{equation*}
		e^N_2 
		=
		\frac1N\sum_{i=1}^N 
		\mathbb{E}\Big[\bar h^N({\cal X}^{i,N}_T)\1{\|{\cal X}^{i,N}_T\|\le k}\1{\tau^{i,N}_R\le T}\Big] 
		+ 
		\frac1N\sum_{i=1}^N\mathbb{E}\Big[\bar h^N({\cal X}^{i,N}_T)\1{\|{\cal X}^{i,N}_T\|\le k}\1{\tau^{i,N}_R> T}\Big]
		\eqqcolon
		e^N_{2,1} + e^N_{2,2}.
	\end{equation*}
	
Let us estimate $e^N_{2,1}$.
	Since $\{\tau^{i,N}_R\le T\} = \{\sup_{t\le T}\|{\cal X}^{i,N}_t\|\ge R\}\cup \{\sup_{t\le T}M^N_t\ge R\}$, subsequently using Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, \eqref{eq.growth.sumhN} and \eqref{eq.e1.exp}, Markov's inequality, and \eqref{eq.estimate.average}, it follows that there is a constant $C>0$, independent of $N,k, R$, which may change from line to line, such that
	\begin{align*}
		| e^N_{2,1} |
		&\le 
		\Big(
		\frac1N\sum_{i=1}^N
		\mathbb{E}\big[
		|\bar h^N({\cal X}^{i,N}_T)|^2
		\big]\Big)^{1/2}
		\Big(\frac1N\sum_{i=1}^N
		\mathbb{P}(\tau^{N,i}_R\le T)
		\Big)^{1/2}\\
		&\le 
		C\Big(
		\frac1N\sum_{i=1}^N
		\mathbb{P}(\tau^{N,i}_R\le T)
		\Big)^{1/2}\\
		& \le 
		\frac{C}{R}
		\Big(\frac1N\sum_{i=1}^N
		\mathbb{E}\Big[\sup_{t\in [0, T]}\|{\cal X}^{i,N}_t\|^2\Big]\Big)^{1/2}
		\le \frac{C}{R}.
	\end{align*}
                                                                     
                                                                                         
                                                                      
                
                   
                                   
                 
  
          
  
  
               
	We now estimate $e^N_{2,2}$.
	                                                
	                                      
	Let 
	\begin{equation*}
	 	\frac{{\mathrm{d}}\mathbb{Q}^{\text{\fontsize{5}{5}\selectfont$i$}\!,\text{\fontsize{5}{5}\selectfont$N$}\!,\text{\fontsize{5}{5}\selectfont$R$}}}{{\mathrm{d}} \mathbb{P}} 
		\coloneqq
		{\cal E}\bigg(-\int_0^\cdot \vartheta^{\text{\fontsize{5}{5}\selectfont$i$}\!,\text{\fontsize{5}{5}\selectfont$N$}\!,\text{\fontsize{5}{5}\selectfont$R$}}_s{\mathrm{d}} B^i_s \bigg )_T, 
		            
		\mbox{ where, }
		\vartheta^{\text{\fontsize{5}{5}\selectfont$i$}\!,\text{\fontsize{5}{5}\selectfont$N$}\!,\text{\fontsize{5}{5}\selectfont$R$}}_t
		\coloneqq 
		\sigma^\dagger
		                   
		\beta^{i,N}_t\1{t\le \tau^{i,N}_R}.
	 \end{equation*}
	Since under                                  
	\Cref{assump.Df}\ref{assump.Df.1} we have $\|\vartheta^{\text{\fontsize{5}{5}\selectfont$i$}\!,\text{\fontsize{5}{5}\selectfont$N$}\!,\text{\fontsize{5}{5}\selectfont$R$}}_t\|^2\le K^2_R/\Lambda$,
	it follows that $\mathbb{Q}^{\text{\fontsize{5}{5}\selectfont$i$}\!,\text{\fontsize{5}{5}\selectfont$N$}\!,\text{\fontsize{5}{5}\selectfont$R$}}$ is a probability measure equivalent to $\mathbb{P}$ and by Girsanov's theorem 
	$\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{B}\overline{\phantom{B}}$}B^i_t
	\coloneqq  
	B^i_t 
	+ 
	\int_0^t\vartheta^{\text{\fontsize{5}{5}\selectfont$i$}\!,\text{\fontsize{5}{5}\selectfont$N$}\!,\text{\fontsize{5}{5}\selectfont$R$}}_s\,\mathrm{d} s
	$ 
	is a $\mathbb{Q}^{i,N,R}$-Brownian motion.
	 Moreover, since $\sigma\vartheta^{\text{\fontsize{5}{5}\selectfont$i$}\!,\text{\fontsize{5}{5}\selectfont$N$}\!,\text{\fontsize{5}{5}\selectfont$R$}}_t = \beta^{i,N}_t\1{t\le \tau^{i,N}_R}$, we have that
	 \begin{equation*}
	 	{\mathrm{d}}{\cal X}^{i,N}_t = \beta^{i,N}_t\1{t >\tau^{i,N}_R}\,\mathrm{d} t + \sigma \,\mathrm{d} \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{B}\overline{\phantom{B}}$}B^i_t, \; \mathbb{Q}^{\text{\fontsize{5}{5}\selectfont$i$}\!,\text{\fontsize{5}{5}\selectfont$N$}\!,\text{\fontsize{5}{5}\selectfont$R$}}\text{--a.s.}
	 \end{equation*}
	 Consequently, 
	 \[
	 X^{i,N}_T = X^i_0 + \sigma \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{B}\overline{\phantom{B}}$}B^i_T, \mbox{ on } \{\tau^{i,N}_R>T\}, \mbox{ and, } X^i_0 + \sigma \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{B}\overline{\phantom{B}}$}B^i_T \mbox{ has density }\nu_{0} \ast \gamma_{\sigma,T},
	 \]
