	 where                                                                            
	                                                     
	 $\gamma_{\sigma,T}$ denotes the density function of a Gaussian random variable with parameters $0$ and $\sigma\sigma^\top T$.
	 Indeed, $X^i_0$ has law $\nu_{0}$ and $\sigma \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{B}\overline{\phantom{B}}$}B^i_T$ is an independent Gaussian with density $\gamma_{\sigma,T}$.
	 Notice that                                                                                                                                                                     
	     
	$\|\nu_{0}\ast\gamma_{\sigma,T}\|_\infty\le C_{\sigma,T} \coloneqq \frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi \Lambda T}}$.\medskip
	 
	 
	 On the other hand, for every $q\ge1$, we have that
	 \begin{align*}
	 	\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big(
		\frac{{\mathrm{d}}\mathbb{P}}{{\mathrm{d}}\mathbb{Q}^{\text{\fontsize{5}{5}\selectfont$i$}\!,\text{\fontsize{5}{5}\selectfont$N$}\!,\text{\fontsize{5}{5}\selectfont$R$}}}
		\Big)^q\Big] 
		&= 
		\mathbb{E}\bigg[{\cal E}\Big(
		q\int_0^\cdot \vartheta^{\text{\fontsize{5}{5}\selectfont$i$}\!,\text{\fontsize{5}{5}\selectfont$N$}\!,\text{\fontsize{5}{5}\selectfont$R$}}_t\,\mathrm{d} \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{B}\overline{\phantom{B}}$}B^i_t
		\Big)_T
		\exp\Big(
		\frac{q(q-1)}{2}
		\int_0^T\|\vartheta^{\text{\fontsize{5}{5}\selectfont$i$}\!,\text{\fontsize{5}{5}\selectfont$N$}\!,\text{\fontsize{5}{5}\selectfont$R$}}_t\|^2 \,\mathrm{d} t
		\Big)\bigg]\\
	 	&\le 
		\mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{Q}^{\text{\fontsize{5}{5}\selectfont$i$}\!,\text{\fontsize{5}{5}\selectfont$N$}\!,\text{\fontsize{5}{5}\selectfont$R$}}}\bigg[
		\exp\Big(
		\frac{q(q-1)}{2}
		\int_0^T\|\vartheta^{\text{\fontsize{5}{5}\selectfont$i$}\!,\text{\fontsize{5}{5}\selectfont$N$}\!,\text{\fontsize{5}{5}\selectfont$R$}}_t\|^2 \,\mathrm{d} t
		\Big)\bigg]\\
		&\leq 
		\exp\Big(\frac{q(q-1)}{2\Lambda}K^2_RT\Big) 
		\eqqcolon C_{q,R,T}.
	\end{align*}
	
With this, we may use H\"older's inequality, first with $p = \frac{q}{q-1}$ and then with conjugates $p^\prime,q^\prime$ such that $pp^\prime<2$, to estimate that                                 
	\begin{align*}
		|e^N_{2,2}| 
		&= 
		\bigg | \frac1N\sum_{i=1}^N
		\mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{Q}^{\text{\fontsize{5}{5}\selectfont$i$}\!,\text{\fontsize{5}{5}\selectfont$N$}\!,\text{\fontsize{5}{5}\selectfont$R$}}}\bigg[
		\frac{{\mathrm{d}} \mathbb{P}}{{\mathrm{d}} \mathbb{Q}^{\text{\fontsize{5}{5}\selectfont$i$}\!,\text{\fontsize{5}{5}\selectfont$N$}\!,\text{\fontsize{5}{5}\selectfont$R$}}}
		\bar h^N({\cal X}^{i,N}_T) \1{\|{\cal X}^{i,N}_\text{\fontsize{4}{4}\selectfont$T$} \|\le k}\1{\tau^{i,N}_R>T} 
		\bigg ]\bigg| \\
		&\le 
		C_{q,R,T}^{1/q}\frac1N\sum_{i=1}^N
		\mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{Q}^{\text{\fontsize{5}{5}\selectfont$i$}\!,\text{\fontsize{5}{5}\selectfont$N$}\!,\text{\fontsize{5}{5}\selectfont$R$}}}
		\Big[
		|\bar h^N(X^i_0 + \sigma \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{B}\overline{\phantom{B}}$}B^i_T)|^p
		 \1{\|X^i_\text{\fontsize{4}{4}\selectfont$0$} + \sigma \bar{B}^i_\text{\fontsize{4}{4}\selectfont$T$}\|\le k}
		 \Big]^{1/p}\\
		&\le 
		C_{q,R,T}^{1/q}
		\bigg(\int_{\mathbb{R}^m}
		|\bar h^N(x)|^p \1{\|x\|\le k} \nu_{0}\ast\gamma_{\sigma,T}(x)\,\mathrm{d} x
		\bigg)^{1/p}\\
	 	&\le 
		C_{q,R,T}^{1/q}
		C_{\sigma,T}^{1/p}
		\bigg(\int_{\mathbb{R}^m} 
		|\bar h^N(x)|^p\1{\|x\|\le k} w_T^{-1}(x) 
		\nu_{T}({\mathrm{d}} x)
		\bigg)^{1/p}\\
	 	&\le 
		C_{q,R,T}^{1/q}C_{\sigma,T}^{1/p}
		\bigg(\int_{B_k}
		               
		w_T^{-q^\prime}(x)\nu_{T}({\mathrm{d}} x)\bigg)^{\frac{1}{pq^\prime}}
		\|\bar h^N\|_{\mathbb{L}^{pp^\prime}(\mathbb{R},\nu_{T})}.
	 	                                       
	 \end{align*} 
	                                                             
	                                                                                     
	
Since, under \Cref{assump.Df}\ref{assump.Df.5} and $B_k$ is compact we have $ \int_{B_k} w_T^{-q^\prime}(x)\nu_{T}({\mathrm{d}} x)<\infty$.
This allows to deduce that there is a constant $C_{R,k}$ independent of $N$, such that 
	\begin{equation}
	\label{eq.e2}
	e^{N}_{2,2}\le C_{R,k}\|\bar h^N\|_{\mathbb{L}^{pp^\prime}(\mathbb{R},\nu_{T})}.
	\end{equation}


                                                                                                                                                 
                                                                                                                                                                                                              
                 
             
      
                                                        
                             
                            
                                                                        
           
            
                     
                          
                                                                    
               
                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   
                 
           
         
                                                              
                 
                                                                       
       
                              
                                                       
               


                 


                                                                                                                                        
                                                               
                                                                                                                                              
                                            
                   
          
          
                                                                                                                             
        
                                                                                                    
                             
                 
                                                                                                                                                                  
                                              
              
                                                 
                                                                                                                                 
                  
                   
              
          
                                                            
                          
                                                                        
                 
                                                                                    
                   
                                                                         
            
                                                                                                                                                                  
                                                                                                                        
                                                                                                                                                                                                 
                 
                                                                                                              
                                                    
                   
                                                                                                
                 
                                                  
                  
                           
                                                      
                
                             
                                 
                                                                                                                                       
                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                
                                                        
                  
           
              
           
                                  
                                                   
          
                                                                                                    
                           
                                                                                                           
                                                                                                     
                
                                                                                                   
                                                                                                 
                                                                 
                                                                                                                                                            
                             
            
        
                                                                                  
    
                                                                          
           
      
                
                                                               
              
                                                                                                                                                
              
                                                                

\medskip
 


{\bf Step 5.} \emph{Finishing the proof.}
By \eqref{eq:maximizing.seq.particle.NEW} and Steps 3 and 4 of this proof, see \eqref{eq.e1N.final} and \eqref{eq.e2}, we have that for any $k>0$ there are constants $C$, independent of $N, R$ and $k$, and $C_{R,k}$ independent of $N$, such that
\begin{align*}
 	\frac{1}{N}V^{N}_e(\nu_{0},\nu_{T})
		& \le 
		\mathop{\mathrm{inf}\vphantom{\mathrm{sup}}}_{\boldsymbol{\alpha} \in \Gamma^N (\nu_{0}^{\otimes N})} 		\frac1N\sum_{i=1}^N
		\mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{P}^{N,\boldsymbol{\alpha}}}\bigg[ 
		\int_0^T\frac12|\alpha^i_t|^2\,\mathrm{d} t + \bar\varphi(X^{i,N}_T)
		\bigg] 
		- 
		\langle 
		\bar \varphi^{N}, \nu_{T} \rangle
		+ 
		\frac{\varepsilon}{N} 
		+
		 e^N_{1} 
		 + 
		 e^N_{2,1} 
		 + 
		 e^N_{2,2} \\
		& \le 
		\mathop{\mathrm{inf}\vphantom{\mathrm{sup}}}_{\boldsymbol{\alpha} \in \Gamma^N (\nu_{0}^{\otimes N})} 		\frac1N\sum_{i=1}^N
		\mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{P}^{N,\boldsymbol{\alpha}}}\bigg[ 
		\int_0^T\frac12|\alpha^i_t|^2\,\mathrm{d} t + \bar\varphi(X^{i,N}_T)
		\bigg] 
		- 
		\langle 
		\bar \varphi^{N}, \nu_{T} \rangle
		+ \frac{\varepsilon}{N} + \frac{C}{k} + \frac{C}{R^{1/2}} + C_{R,k}\|\bar h^N\|_{\mathbb{L}^{pp'}(\mathbb{R},\nu_{T})}.
                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   
 \end{align*} 
                                                                    
                                                                                                                                                    
                                                                                    
                                                                                                                                                                                               
                                                                                                                                                                                               
                                               
                                                                                                                                                
                 
                                  
                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               
                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  
                                                                                              
                
 Thus, thanks to \Cref{prop.conv.phi^N}, taking the $\limsup$ above as $N$ goes to infinity for fixed $R,k$, and then as $R$ and $k$ go to infinity we find that
                                                                                                                                                                                                              
                                                                                                                                         
\begin{align*}
	\limsup_{N\longrightarrow \infty}\frac1NV^N_e(\nu_{0},\nu_{T}) &\le \mathop{\mathrm{inf}\vphantom{\mathrm{sup}}}_{\alpha\in \Gamma(\nu_{0})}\mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{P}^\alpha }\bigg[\int_0^T\frac12\|\alpha_t\|^2\,\mathrm{d} t +     
	\bar\varphi( X_T)\bigg]  -\langle \bar\varphi,\nu_{T}\rangle 
	    
	                                                                                                                                      
	                     
\end{align*}
where the convergence of the first term is due to \cite[Theorems 1 and 2 and Proposition 1]{djete2020mckean2} and that of the second and last terms are due to \Cref{prop.conv.phi^N}.
                                                                  
                                                               
              
That is, we have
\begin{equation}
	\label{eq:prof.conv.last.bound}
	\limsup_{N\longrightarrow \infty}\frac1NV^N_e(\nu_{0},\nu_{T}) \le \Psi^{\bar\varphi}(\nu_{0}) - \langle \bar\varphi, \nu_{T} \rangle 
\end{equation}
                                                             
where the function $\Psi^\phi$ was introduced in the statement of Theorem \ref{thm:duality.gen}.
This yields the desired bound and concludes the proof.
<!-- end proof-12 -->
<!-- proof-13: body_md -->
From \eqref{eq:prof.conv.last.bound} in the proof of Theorem \ref{thm:parti.conv.N}, we have
	\begin{align*}
		V_e(\nu_{0},\nu_{T})\le \liminf_{N\longrightarrow\infty}\frac1NV^N_e(\nu_{0},\nu_{T})\le \limsup_{N\longrightarrow\infty}\frac1NV^N_e(\nu_{0},\nu_{T})\le \Psi^{\bar\varphi}(\nu_{0})- \langle \bar\varphi,\nu_{T}\rangle \le V_e(\nu_{0},\nu_{T}).
	\end{align*}
	This shows that $\bar\varphi\in H^1(\mathbb{R}^m, \nu_{T})$ is optimal for $V_e$.
<!-- end proof-13 -->
<!-- proof-14: body_md -->
Let us argue the equivalence first. 
	The proof relies on a known verification argument for McKean-Vlasov optimal control problems.
	By \cite[Proposition 3.7]{djete2019mckean}, a sufficiently regular solution $v$ of the HJB equation satisfifes $v(0, \nu_{0})\le J^\phi(\gamma)$ for every admissible rule $\gamma$, with equality if and only if the control minimizes the integrated Hamiltonian ${\mathrm{d}} t\otimes {\mathrm{d}}\mathbb{P}^\gamma$-\text{--a.s.} 
	                                                                                                                                                          
	Therein, the result is stated in terms of the Hamiltonian operator $\hat H$, defined in terms of the integrated map $\hat h$ evaluated at a sufficiently smooth function $v(t,\mu)$, as opposed to the integral of the pointwise Hamiltonian, which is the setting in \Cref{theorem.verification}. 
	That is, for $v \in {\cal C}^{1,2}_{\rm flat}$, \cite[Theorem 3.7]{djete2019mckean} considers
	\[
	\hat H[v] (t,\mu)\coloneqq  \mathop{\mathrm{inf}\vphantom{\mathrm{sup}}}_{u\in \mathbb{L}^2(\mu)} \hat h[v] (t,\mu,u), \; \mbox{ where } \hat h[v] (t,\mu,u)\coloneqq  \int_{\mathbb{R}^m} h(t,x,\mu,\nabla \delta v(t,\mu)(x), \nabla^2 \delta v(t,\mu)(x),  u(x) ) \mu( {\mathrm{d}} x),
	\]
and where the infimum is over $\mathbb{L}^2(\mu)$, the collection of $\mu$-square integrable maps $u:(\mathbb{R}^m,\mathcal{B}(\mathbb{R}^m),\mu) \longrightarrow {\cal U}$.                                                                                                                   
\medskip

Therefore, let us argue that
\begin{align}\label{eq.ver.int.ham}
\hat H[v] (t,\mu)= \int_{\mathbb{R}^m}  \bigg(  \mathop{\mathrm{inf}\vphantom{\mathrm{sup}}}_{{\bf u} \in {\cal U} }  h(t,x,\mu,\nabla \delta v(t,\mu)(x), \nabla^2 \delta v(t,\mu)(x),  {\bf u} )\bigg)  \mu({\mathrm{d}} x).
\end{align}
	But this is precisely the content of \cite[Theorem 14.60]{rockafellar2009variational} which allows to interchange infimum and integration a decomposable function class and a normal integrand.
	Note that $ \mathbb{L}^2(\mu)$ is decomposable relative to $\mu$ (see the discussion preceding \cite[Theorem 14.60]{rockafellar2009variational}) and since $v \in {\cal C}^{1,2}_{\rm flat}$ and by the growth condition on $f$, it holds $\hat h[v](t,\mu,u)<+\infty$ on $\mathbb{L}^2(\mu)$.\medskip
	
                                                                                                                                             
                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           
	
                                                                                                    
                                                                                                                                                                                                          
	
	Let $(t,\mu)\in [0,T]\times {\cal P}_2(\mathbb{R}^m)$ be fixed, $\mathbb{R}^m\times( \mathbb{R}^m\times\mathbb{S}^m) \ni  (x,{\bf u})\longmapsto h_{t,\mu}(x,\boldsymbol u )\coloneqq h(t,x,\mu,\nabla \delta v(t,\mu)(x), \nabla^2 \delta v(t,\mu)(x), {\bf u} )$ and $\chi_{{\cal U}}({\bf u})\coloneqq  0 \1{{\cal U}}({\bf u})+\infty \1{{\cal U}^c}({\bf u})$ denote the indicator of the set ${\cal U}$.
	Notice that, $x\longmapsto h_{t,\mu}(x,\boldsymbol u)$ is measurable, and, $\boldsymbol{u}\longmapsto h_{t,\mu}(x,\boldsymbol{u})$ is continuous since $f$ and the other terms defining $h$ are continuous in $\boldsymbol{u}$.
	Thus, thanks to \cite[Example 14.32]{rockafellar2009variational}, the function $\mathbb{R}^m\times( \mathbb{R}^m\times\mathbb{S}^m) \ni  (x,{\bf u})\longmapsto  \tilde h_{t,\mu}(x, {\bf u} )\coloneqq h_{t,\mu}(x,\boldsymbol u ) +\chi_{{\cal U}}({\bf u})$ is a normal integral,
  
  
                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         
                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          
                                                                                                                                   
  
	and thanks to \cite[Theorem 14.60]{rockafellar2009variational},
	\begin{align*}
	\hat H[v] (t,\mu)  =  \mathop{\mathrm{inf}\vphantom{\mathrm{sup}}}_{u\in \mathbb{L}^2(\mu)}  \int_{\mathbb{R}^m} \tilde h_{t,\mu}(x, u(x) ) \mu( {\mathrm{d}} x) 
	& =  \int_{\mathbb{R}^m}  \bigg(  \mathop{\mathrm{inf}\vphantom{\mathrm{sup}}}_{{\bf u} \in \mathbb{R}^m\times \mathbb{S}^m }  \tilde h_{t,\mu}(x, {\bf u} ) \bigg)  \mu({\mathrm{d}} x)\\
	& =  \int_{\mathbb{R}^m}  \bigg(  \mathop{\mathrm{inf}\vphantom{\mathrm{sup}}}_{{\bf u} \in {\cal U} }  h(t,x,\mu,\nabla \delta v(t,\mu)(x), \nabla^2 \delta v(t,\mu)(x),  {\bf u} )\bigg)  \mu({\mathrm{d}} x),
	\end{align*}
where the first and last equalities follow by definition of $\tilde h_{t,\mu}$ and the second is \cite[Theorem 14.60]{rockafellar2009variational}.	
	That is, \eqref{eq.ver.int.ham} holds and, moreover, the minimizers are in correspondence,
	\begin{equation}
	\label{eq:meas.selec.optim}
	\boldsymbol{u}^\star(t,\mu) \in \operatorname*{arg\,min}_{u\in \mathbb{L}^2(\mu)} \hat h[v](t,\mu,u) \Longleftrightarrow \boldsymbol{u}^\star(t,x ) \in \operatorname*{arg\,min}_{{\bf u}\in {\cal U} }  h(t,x,\mu,\nabla \delta v(t,\mu)(x), \nabla^2 \delta v(t,\mu)(x),  {\bf u} ), \mu\text{--a.e.}
	\end{equation}
	{\color{black}In conclusion, if $(i)$ holds, then by \eqref{eq:meas.selec.optim} $\hat H[v](t,\mu^\star_t) = \hat h[v](t,\mu^\star_t,u^\star(t,\mu^\star_t))$ $\mu_t^\star$\text{--a.s.}, thus by \cite[Proposition 3.7]{djete2019mckean} it holds $\boldsymbol{u}^\star(t, X^\star_t)$ is optimal.
	Conversely, if $(ii)$ holds, then by \cite[Proposition 3.7]{djete2019mckean}, $\hat H[v](t,\mu^\star_t) = \hat h[v](t,\mu^\star_t,u^\star(t,\mu^\star_t))$ and using \eqref{eq:meas.selec.optim} and the fact that $\mu^\star_t = \mathbb{P}^\star\circ (X_t^\star)^{-1}$, we have $(i)$.}

\medskip

We are left only with the last two statements.
	Let $c\equiv \int_{\mathbb{R}^m} \1{x\neq y} \rho({\mathrm{d}} y)$.
	First, let $\varphi^\star$ be a mean field potential, $v^{\varphi^\star\!\!}$~be a solution to \eqref{eq:HJB}, and $\gamma^\star\in \Gamma_{\rm det} (\nu_{0},\nu_{T})$ be such that $(i)$ holds.
	It follows from the established equivalence that 
	\[ V_c(\nu_{0},\nu_{T})= \Psi^{\varphi^\star}(\nu_{0}) -\langle \varphi^\star,\nu_{T}\rangle=\mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{P}^\star}\bigg[\int_0^Tf(t,X^\star_t,u^\star_t, \mu^\star_t) \,\mathrm{d} t\bigg] +\langle \varphi^\star,\mathbb{P}^\star_T\rangle -\langle \varphi^\star,\nu_{T}\rangle=\mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{P}^\star}\bigg[\int_0^Tf(t,X^\star_t,u^\star_t, \mu^\star_t) \,\mathrm{d} t\bigg] .	\]
	Conversely, let $\gamma^\star\in \Gamma_{\rm det} (\nu_{0},\nu_{T})$ be primal optimal, $\varphi^\star$ be such that $(i)$ holds with $v^{\varphi^\star\!\!}$ solution to \eqref{eq:HJB}. Then
	\[ \Psi^{\varphi^\star}(\nu_{0}) -\langle \varphi^\star,\nu_{T}\rangle= v^{\varphi^\star\!\!}(\nu_{0}) -\langle \varphi^\star,\nu_{T}\rangle = \mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{P}^\star}\bigg[\int_0^Tf(t,X^\star_t,u^\star_t, \mu^\star_t) \,\mathrm{d} t\bigg] = V_e(\nu_{0},\nu_{T}).\qedhere
	\]
<!-- end proof-14 -->
<!-- proof-15: body_md -->
Since $v\in {\cal C}^{1,2,{\rm full}}_{\rm flat}$, to ease the notation, we set 
\[
	u(t,\mu)(x)\coloneqq \frac{\delta v}{\delta \mu}(t,\mu)(x) \mbox{ and } w(t,\mu)(y;x)\coloneqq \frac{\delta^2 v}{\delta \mu^2}(t,\mu)(y;x).
\]
Thus, since $v$ is a solution to \eqref{eq:HJB} with datum $\varphi$, we have that 
\begin{equation}\label{eq:HJBBCGL}
\begin{cases}
	\partial_tv(t,\mu)
	+
	\int_{\mathbb{R}^m} \Big(
	\tfrac12 \Delta u(t,\mu)(x)
	-
	\nabla V \ast \mu(x) \cdot \nabla u(t,\mu)(x)
	-
	\tfrac12 \big| \nabla u(t,\mu)(x) \big|^2 
	\Big)\mu({\mathrm{d}} x)
	=0, 
	\; (t,\mu) \in [0,T]\times {\cal P}_2(\mathbb{R}^m) \\
v(T,\mu)=\int\varphi\,{\mathrm{d}}\mu  \mbox{ for all } \mu \in {\cal P}_2(\mathbb{R}^m).
\end{cases}
\end{equation}
Moreover, for any smooth function $g$, we introduce $(\mathcal A_tg)(y)\coloneqq \tfrac12\Delta g(y)-\big(\nabla V\ast \mu^\star_t(y)+\nabla  u(t, \mu^\star_t)( y) \big)\cdot\nabla g(y)$. 
	We argue in four steps.\medskip

{\bf Step 1.} We collect some results from the flat calculus. 
	Let $t\in [0,T]$.
	The flat derivative of the map $\mu\longmapsto\int G(t,y,\mu)\,\mu({\mathrm{d}} y)$, with $G$ built from $u$,
	$\nabla_yu$, $\Delta_yu$ and $\nabla W\ast\mu$ as in \eqref{eq:HJBBCGL}, exists and, up to the normalizing constant, satisfies
	\begin{equation}\label{eq:productrule}
	\frac{\delta}{\delta\mu}\Big(\int G(t,y,\cdot)\, \mu({\mathrm{d}} y)\Big)(x)
	=
	G(t,x,\mu)+\int\frac{\delta G}{\delta\mu}(t,y,\mu)(x)\,\mu({\mathrm{d}} y), \; x\in \mathbb{R}^m. 
	                                                                                                 
	\end{equation}
Moreover, 
	\begin{equation}\label{eq:step3}
	\int\mathcal A_t\big(w(t, \mu^\star_t)(\cdot ;x)\big)(y)\,\mu^\star_t({\mathrm{d}} y)
	=
	\int\mathcal A_t\big(w(t, \mu^\star_t)(x;\cdot )\big)(y) \,\mu^\star_t({\mathrm{d}} y)
	+
	\int\mathcal A_t \big(  u(t, \mu^\star_t)\big) ( y)  \,\mu^\star_t({\mathrm{d}} y), \; x\in \mathbb{R}^m. 
	\end{equation}
Indeed, \eqref{eq:productrule} is the product rule for the flat derivative, which holds since $v\in {\cal C}^{1,2,{\rm full}}_{\rm flat}$.
	Let us now argue \eqref{eq:step3}.
	Differentiating the normalization $\int u(t,\mu)(z)\,\mu({\mathrm{d}} z)=0$ in $\mu$ in the direction $x$, we find thanks to \eqref{eq:productrule} that 
	\(
	\int w(t,\mu)  (z;x)\,\mu({\mathrm{d}} z)=-u(t,\mu) (x).
	\)
	Let $x,y\in\mathbb{R}^m$, and note that, since $v\in {\cal C}^{1,2,{\rm full}}_{\rm flat}$, the map $[0,1]^2\ni (\lambda,\lambda^\prime)\overset{g}{\longmapsto} v\big(t,\mu+\lambda(\delta_x-\mu)+\lambda^\prime(\delta_y-\mu)\big)$,
is $C^2$ on $[0,1]^2\cap \{\lambda+\lambda^\prime \le1\}$, with continuous crossed partial derivatives, and thus they agree at $(0,0)$.
	Computing them from the definition of $u$ and $w$ and using the two normalizations, we find that
	\(
	(\partial_{\lambda \lambda^\prime}^2g)(0,0)
	=
	w(y;x)+u(x)
	,\, 
	(\partial_{ \lambda^\prime\lambda}^2g)(0,0)
	=
	w(x;y)+u(y).
	\)
	That is,
	\[
	w(t,\mu) (y;x)
	=
	w(t,\mu) (x;y)+u(t,\mu)  (y)-u(t,\mu)  (x) \mbox{ for all }x,y\in\mathbb{R}^m.
	\]
	{\color{black}Notice that $w$ is not symmetric here because the flat derivative $u$ is defined by the normalization $\int u(t,\mu)(z)\mu({\mathrm{d}} z) =0$.
	The preceding identity is the corresponding symmetry relation for the second derivative.}
	With this, \eqref{eq:step3} follows applying $\mathcal A_t$ in the variable $y$ above at $\mu=\mu^\star_t$ and integrating with respect to $\mu^\star_t$.
                                                                                                                 
\medskip

{\bf Step 2.} Let $U(t,x)\coloneqq u(t,\mu^\star_t)(x)$. We claim that,                                                      
\begin{equation}\label{eq:step2}
	\partial_t U(t,x)
	=
	\partial_t u(t,\mu^\star_t)(x)
	+
	\int\mathcal A_t\big(w(x;y)\big)\,\mu^\star_t({\mathrm{d}} y),
	 \; (t,x)\in [0,T) \times \mathbb R^m.
\end{equation}
Let  $x\in \mathbb R^m$ be fixed. 
	By assumption, the map $(t,\mu)\longmapsto u(t,\mu)(x)$ belongs to $\mathcal C^{1,2}_{\rm flat}$, with flat derivative $w(t,\mu)(x;\cdot)$ in the space variable.
	Recall that by assumption under $\gamma^\star$ the drift is $-\nabla W\ast \mu^\star_t(X_t^{\star})-\nabla u(t, \mu^\star_t)( X_t^{\star}),\, {\mathrm{d}} t\otimes{\mathrm{d}} \mathbb{P}^{\gamma^\star}\text{--a.e.}$
	Then, \eqref{eq:step2} holds for ${\rm Leb}\text{--a.e.}\; t\in [0,T]$, thanks to \cite[Proposition~5.102]{carmona2018probabilisticI}, which holds since $v\in {\cal C}^{1,2,{\rm full}}_{\rm flat}$, along the flow of marginals $\mu^\star_\cdot$ and the constant process $x$. 
	That the identity holds for every $t\in[0,T)$ follows by continuity of both sides, thanks to the regularity of $v$.
	\medskip

{\bf Step 3.} We claim that there is $\varrho_o:[0,T)\longrightarrow \mathbb{R}$ such that, for any $(t,x)\in [0,T) \times \mathbb R^m$,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:step1}
\begin{aligned}
	&\partial_tu(t,\mu^\star_t)(x) 
		+
		\tfrac12\Delta U(t,x)
		-
		\nabla V \ast\mu^\star_t(x)\cdot\nabla U(t,x)
		-
		\tfrac12|\nabla U(t,x)|^2
		+
		\int\mathcal A_t\big(w(y;x)\big)\,\mu^\star_t({\mathrm{d}} y)\\
	&\; -\int\nabla V(y-x)\cdot\nabla U(t,y)\,\mu^\star_t({\mathrm{d}} y)
		+
		\int \nabla V \ast \mu^\star_t(y)\cdot\nabla U(t,y)\,\mu^\star_t({\mathrm{d}} y)
		=
		\varrho_o(t,\mu^\star_t).
\end{aligned}
\end{equation}
                                                                    
	Let $(t,x)\in [0,T) \times \mathbb R^m$ be fixed.
	For any $(y,\mu) \in \mathbb{R}^m\times {\cal P}(\mathbb{R}^m)$, let  
	\[
	\Phi(t,\mu)\coloneqq \partial_tv(t,\mu)+\int G(t,y,\mu)\,\mu({\mathrm{d}} y),
	\mbox{ and }
	G(t,y,\mu)\coloneqq \tfrac12\Delta_y u(t,\mu)(y) - \nabla V \ast \mu (y) \cdot \nabla_y u(t,\mu)(y)  -\tfrac12 |\nabla_yu(t,\mu)(y) |^2,
                
                   
	\] 
	By \eqref{eq:HJBBCGL}, $\Phi\equiv0$ on $[0,T)\times{\cal P}_2(\mathbb{R}^d)$. 
	Consider the map $[0,1]\ni \lambda\longmapsto\Phi\big(t,(1-\lambda)\mu+\lambda\delta_x\big)$ and note it vanishes on $[0,1]$, with right derivative at $\lambda=0$ equal to $\frac{\delta \Phi}{\delta\mu}(t,\mu)(x)-\int\frac{\delta \Phi}{\delta\mu}(t,\mu)(y) \,\mu({\mathrm{d}} y)$. 
	Thus,
	\begin{equation}\label{eq:dPhi}
	\frac{\delta \Phi}{\delta\mu}(t,\mu)(x)=\varrho_o(t,\mu)\; \mbox{for all }x\in\mathbb{R}^m, \; \varrho_o(t,\mu)\coloneqq \int\frac{\delta \Phi}{\delta\mu}(t,\mu)(y) \mu({\mathrm{d}} y) .
	\end{equation}
	Let us find $\frac{\delta \Phi}{\delta\mu}$. 
	By the commutation property of $v\in {\cal C}^{1,2,{\rm full}}_{\rm flat}$, $\frac{\delta (\partial_tv)}{\delta\mu}=\partial_tu$, and, for $u=u(t,\mu)$, $w=w(t,\mu)$,
	\[
	\frac{\delta G}{\delta\mu}(t,y,\mu)(x)
		=
		\tfrac12\Delta_yw(y;x)
		-
		\big(
		\nabla V\ast \mu(y)
		+
		\nabla_yu(y)
		\big)
		\cdot\nabla_y w(y;x)
		-
		\big(
		\nabla V(y-x)-\nabla V\ast \mu(y)\big)
		\cdot\nabla_yu(y),
	\]
	
	The result follows differentiating in $\Phi\equiv0$, evaluating at $\mu=\mu^\star_t$, where $u(t,\mu^\ast_t)(\cdot)=U(t,\cdot)$, thanks to \eqref{eq:productrule}. 
	                                                                                
	\medskip

{\bf Step 4.} We prove \eqref{eq:BCGL17}.
	Replace \eqref{eq:step3} into \eqref{eq:step1}, and use \eqref{eq:step2} to replace $\partial_tu(t,\mu^\star_t)(x)+\int\mathcal A_t(w(x;y))\,\mu^\star_t({\mathrm{d}} y)$ by $\partial_tU(t,x)$. 
	Notice the $w$-terms cancel and, since $W$ is symmetric, we have that $\nabla V(y-x)=-\nabla V(x-y)$.
	Thus, for all $(t,x)\in[0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^m$,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:linHJB}
	\begin{cases}
	\partial_tU
	+
	\tfrac12\Delta U
	-
	\nabla V \ast \mu^\star_t\cdot\nabla U
	-
	\tfrac12|\nabla U|^2
	+
	\int_{\mathbb{R}^d}\nabla V (x-y)\cdot\nabla U(t,y)\,\mu^\star_t({\mathrm{d}} y)
	=
	\varrho(t),\\
	U(T,\cdot)=\varphi-\int\varphi\,{\mathrm{d}}\mu^\star_T .
	\end{cases}
\end{equation}
	where
	\(
	\varrho(t)\coloneqq \varrho_o(t,\mu^\star_t)-\int\mathcal A_tU(t,y)\,\mu^\star_t({\mathrm{d}} y) -\int\nabla W\ast \mu^\star_t(y) \cdot\nabla U(t,y)\, \mu^\star_t({\mathrm{d}} y) 
	\).
	The terminal condition follows from $u(T,\mu)(x)=\frac{\delta }{\delta\mu}\big(\int\varphi\,{\mathrm{d}}\mu\big)(x)=\varphi(x)-\int\varphi\,{\mathrm{d}}\mu$ due to the normalization. 
	For $\psi$ as in the statement, note that $\nabla\psi=-\nabla U$, $\partial_t\psi=-\partial_tU-\varrho(t)$, and $\psi(T,\cdot)=-\varphi$, which is 
	\eqref{eq:BCGL17}.
<!-- end proof-15 -->
<!-- lemma-4: body_md -->
\label{lem:flowdetermined}
For $t\in[0,T]$, let                                      
$\theta_t:\bar \Omega\longrightarrow \bar\Omega$, 
	$\theta_t(\bar {\mathbf x},\bar {\mathbf u},\bar {\mathbf w})
	\coloneqq (\hat{\mathbf x}_{t\wedge\cdot},\hat{\mathbf u}_{t\wedge\cdot},\hat{\mathbf w})$,
	and 
	\(
	\iota:{\cal P}(\bar \Omega)\longrightarrow
	C([0,T],{\cal P}(\bar \Omega)),
	\iota(\bar \mu)\coloneqq (\bar\mu\circ\theta_t^{-1})_{t\in[0,T]}
	\).
	Then $\iota$ is well defined and continuous, 
	and for every $\mathbb{P}\in{\cal P}(\nu_{0})$,
	\(
		\bar\mu=\iota(\bar\mu_T),\; \mathbb{P}\text{--a.s.}
	\)
\begin{proof}
That $\iota$ is well-defined is clear.
	To prove the continuity, recall that $\mathcal P(\bar \Omega)$ and $C([0,T],{\cal P}(\bar \Omega))$ are endowed with the weak and uniform topologies, respectively.
	Notice first that for fixed $\bar \mu$, $t\longmapsto\bar \mu\circ\theta_t^{-1}$ is continuous.
	Let $\bar \mu^n\longrightarrow \bar \mu$ weakly.
	Then, $\{\bar \mu^n, n\geq 1\}\cup\{\bar \mu\}$ is tight.
	Thus, $\bar\mu^n\circ\theta_t^{-1}\longrightarrow \bar\mu\circ\theta_t^{-1}$, for $t\in [0,T]$ fixed.
	By the equicontinuity of the paths on a compact $K\subseteq\bar\Omega$, we then have that $\bar\mu^n\circ\theta_t^{-1}\longrightarrow \bar\mu\circ\theta_t^{-1}$, uniformly in $t\in [0,T]$.
	That is, $\iota(\bar \mu^n)\longrightarrow \iota(\bar \mu)$ in $C([0,T],\mathcal P(\bar\Omega))$.\medskip

	Let $t\in[0,T]$. By \Cref{def.controlrule}\ref{def.controlrule.3} at $T$ and $t$, both $\bar\mu_t$ and $\bar\mu_T\circ\theta_t^{-1}$ are versions of $\mathcal L^{\mathbb{P}} ((X_{t\wedge\cdot},U_{t\wedge\cdot},W) \, |\, \overline{{\cal G}}_T )$, hence they are equal $\mathbb{P}\text{--a.s.}$
	By continuity of both sides in $t$, $\bar\mu$ and $\iota(\bar \mu_T)=(\bar\mu_T\circ\theta_t^{-1})_{t\in [0,T]}$ agree $\mathbb{P}\text{--a.s.}$
\end{proof}
<!-- end lemma-4 -->
